# Issue Summary: Ideas from Matvey for API/MCP

**Issue #6** was opened on April 4, 2026 by Rob Oudendijk in the Safecast/safecast-new-map repository.

## Core Proposal

The issue presents 15 interconnected recommendations for enhancing Safecast's radioactive measurement analysis system through MCP (Model Context Protocol) integration and LLM capabilities.

## Key Recommendations

**API Architecture**: "Extend the existing API so that the UI and the LLM can call it as a set of structured methods/tools" rather than building separate infrastructure.

**Automatic Analytics**: Implement post-upload processing including track storage, baseline computation, anomaly detection, quality assessment, and result caching.

**Contextual Data Integration**: Connect multiple external sources including geological maps, hydrological data, weather records, fire databases, and spectral signature libraries.

**Environmental Factors**: Account for weather conditions, seasonal variations, and fire-related contamination resuspension and transport mechanisms.

**Contamination Pathways**: Analyze water transport (hydrographic networks, groundwater migration), airborne spread (atmospheric transport, precipitation deposition), and technogenic sources (mines, nuclear facilities, industrial sites).

**Spectrometry Analysis**: Leverage spectral signatures for source attribution and cross-location comparison.

**User Scenario Collection**: Gather personalized exposure information through structured UI/API questioning about residence location, water sources, food consumption, and soil contact.

**Human-Centered Output**: Present findings in accessible language focusing on measured values, natural versus technogenic sources, and potential contamination pathways.
```

# Safecast API/MCP Enhancement Recommendations

This GitHub issue documents 15 strategic suggestions from Matvey for expanding the Safecast project's capabilities:

1. **MCP-Compatible API Layer** – Extend backend API without separate server; prepare structured methods for tracks, context, user scenarios, spectrometry, and interpretation.

2. **Automatic Post-Upload Analytics** – Store measurement points and compute summaries, baselines, anomalies, quality assessments, and cache results.

3. **Contextual Data Integration** – Connect Wikimapia, geological APIs, hydrology systems, terrain data, land use maps, technogenic objects, weather, fire data, and spectral libraries.

4. **Weather/Seasonal Context** – Account for snow, rain, humidity, wind, dust affecting measurements and explain temporary background changes.

5. **Fire Risk Analysis** – Monitor forest and industrial fires as mechanisms for resuspending radioactive particles and transporting contamination.

6. **Geological Assessment** – Determine lithology, natural radiation background, radon potential, and granite/uranium-bearing rock contributions.

7. **Technogenic Source Mapping** – Identify nearby mines, tailings, quarries, nuclear facilities, waste sites, and calculate transport pathways.

8. **Water Contamination Transport** – Analyze hydrographic networks, watershed flow, upstream sources, groundwater migration, and aquifer connectivity.

9. **Atmospheric Transport Analysis** – Account for remote sources, atmospheric deposition, precipitation, dust transport, and support HYSPLIT integration.

10. **High-Risk Point Integration** – Treat elevated measurement points as analytical layers; correlate with technogenic objects, geology, and water context.

11. **Spectrometry Comparison** – Use spectral analysis for source attribution; compare with nearby points, upstream locations, known signatures, and fallout scenarios.

12. **Isotope Interpretation** – Apply isotope-based analysis only with spectrometry; group related pathways (potassium/fertilizers, tritium/water, building materials).

13. **Personalized User Scenario Collection** – Gather behavioral data via UI/API: residency type, water source, food sourcing, soil contact, building materials used.

14. **Exposure Pathway Assessment** – Separately evaluate gamma background, radon, water, groundwater, food chains, inhalation, atmospheric fallout, and residence duration.

15. **User-Friendly Final Response** – Present findings simply: what was measured, plain-language meaning, natural versus technogenic sources, and contamination arrival routes.
```

1	// track_insights.go — GET /api/track/{id}/insights
2	//
3	// Returns cached RAG responses (Q&A pairs with positive feedback) that are
4	// spatially or semantically relevant to the given track, plus curated
5	// location_knowledge notes that fall within the track's bounding box.
6	//
7	// Relevance ranking:
8	//  1. Hard filter: only qa_embeddings with feedback_score > 0.
9	//  2. Semantic similarity: embed "radiation measurements near <centroid>"
10	//     and cosine-compare against stored embeddings (threshold = ragContextThreshold).
11	//  3. Return top 5 by similarity score.
12	//  4. Location notes: location_knowledge rows whose (lat, lon) falls inside bbox.
13	//
14	// Registration: http.HandleFunc("GET /api/track/{id}/insights", trackInsightsHandler)
15	// in mcp_register.go, registered on http.DefaultServeMux (port 8765).
16	
17	package main
18	
19	import (
20		"context"
21		"database/sql"
22		"encoding/json"
23		"fmt"
24		"net/http"
25		"sort"
26	
27		"safecast-new-map/pkg/database"
28	)
29	
30	// trackInsight is a single cached Q&A relevant to the track.
31	type trackInsight struct {
32		ChatID          int64   `json:"chat_id"`
33		Question        string  `json:"question"`
34		Answer          string  `json:"answer"`
35		SimilarityScore float32 `json:"similarity_score"`
36		FeedbackScore   int     `json:"feedback_score"`
37	}
38	
39	// trackLocationNote is a curated geographic note near the track.
40	type trackLocationNote struct {
41		Lat           float64 `json:"lat"`
42		Lon           float64 `json:"lon"`
43		Note          string  `json:"note"`
44		SourceChatID  int64   `json:"source_chat_id,omitempty"`
45		FeedbackScore int     `json:"feedback_score"`
46	}
47	
48	// trackInsightsResponse is the JSON payload for GET /api/track/{id}/insights.
49	type trackInsightsResponse struct {
50		TrackID       string              `json:"track_id"`
51		Insights      []trackInsight      `json:"insights"`
52		LocationNotes []trackLocationNote `json:"location_notes"`
53	}
54	
55	// trackInsightsHandler serves GET /api/track/{id}/insights.
56	// Registered on http.DefaultServeMux via mcp_register.go using Go 1.22 pattern routing.
57	func trackInsightsHandler(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
58		ctx := r.Context()
59		trackID := r.PathValue("id")
60		if trackID == "" {
61			writeError(w, http.StatusBadRequest, "track id required")
62			return
63		}
64	
65		if !dbAvailable() {
66			writeError(w, http.StatusServiceUnavailable, "database not available")
67			return
68		}
69	
70		// 1. Load bounding box from PostgreSQL.
71		bounds, found, err := db.GetTrackBounds(ctx, trackID)
72		if err != nil {
73			writeError(w, http.StatusInternalServerError, "could not load track bounds")
74			return
75		}
76		if !found {
77			writeError(w, http.StatusNotFound, "track not found")
78			return
79		}
80	
81		resp := trackInsightsResponse{
82			TrackID:       trackID,
83			Insights:      insightsByEmbedding(ctx, bounds, trackID),
84			LocationNotes: locationNotesInBbox(bounds, trackID),
85		}
86		w.Header().Set("Content-Type", "application/json")
87		json.NewEncoder(w).Encode(resp) //nolint:errcheck
88	}
89	
90	// insightsByEmbedding returns the top-5 positively-rated Q&A pairs whose
91	// embeddings are most similar to a query generated from the track centroid.
92	func insightsByEmbedding(ctx context.Context, bounds database.Bounds, trackID string) []trackInsight {
93		out := []trackInsight{}
94		if !duckDBAvailable() {
95			return out
96		}
97	
98		centerLat := (bounds.MinLat + bounds.MaxLat) / 2
99		centerLon := (bounds.MinLon + bounds.MaxLon) / 2
100		autoQuery := fmt.Sprintf("radiation measurements near %.3f %.3f", centerLat, centerLon)
101	
102		// getEmbedding ignores ctx (local feature-hash implementation).
103		embedding, err := getEmbedding(ctx, autoQuery)
104		if err != nil || len(embedding) == 0 {
105			return out
106		}
107	
108		// Load only Q&A that reference this specific track ID so the sidebar
109		// doesn't show insights from unrelated tracks.
110		entries, err := loadQAEmbeddingsForTrack(trackID)
111		if err != nil {
112			return out
113		}
114	
115		type scored struct {
116			e     qaEntry
117			score float32
118		}
119		// Include ALL positively-rated entries; the centroid auto-query is too
120		// generic to reach ragContextThreshold (0.50) against natural-language
121		// questions. We still rank by similarity so the most topically relevant
122		// results float to the top.
123		var candidates []scored
124		for _, e := range entries {
125			candidates = append(candidates, scored{e, cosineSimilarity(embedding, e.Embedding)})
126		}
127	
128		// Sort: primary = feedback_score descending, tie-break = cosine similarity descending.
129		sort.Slice(candidates, func(i, j int) bool {
130			fi, fj := candidates[i].e.FeedbackScore, candidates[j].e.FeedbackScore
131			if fi != fj {
132				return fi > fj
133			}
134			return candidates[i].score > candidates[j].score
135		})
136	
137		limit := 5
138		if len(candidates) < limit {
139			limit = len(candidates)
140		}
141	
142		for _, c := range candidates[:limit] {
143			out = append(out, trackInsight{
144				ChatID:          c.e.ChatID,
145				Question:        c.e.Question,
146				Answer:          c.e.Answer,
147				SimilarityScore: c.score,
148				FeedbackScore:   c.e.FeedbackScore,
149			})
150		}
151		return out
152	}
153	
154	// locationNotesInBbox returns location_knowledge rows whose point falls
155	// inside the track bounding box.
156	func locationNotesInBbox(bounds database.Bounds, trackID string) []trackLocationNote {
157		out := []trackLocationNote{}
158		if !duckDBAvailable() {
159			return out
160		}
161	
162		rows, err := duckDB.Query(
163			`SELECT lk.lat, lk.lon, lk.note, lk.source_chat_id,
164			        COALESCE(qe.feedback_score, 0) AS feedback_score
165			 FROM location_knowledge lk
166			 LEFT JOIN qa_embeddings qe ON qe.chat_id = lk.source_chat_id
167			 WHERE lk.lat BETWEEN ? AND ? AND lk.lon BETWEEN ? AND ?
168			 ORDER BY feedback_score DESC, lk.created_at DESC LIMIT 20`,
169			bounds.MinLat, bounds.MaxLat, bounds.MinLon, bounds.MaxLon,
170		)
171		if err != nil {
172			return out
173		}
174		defer rows.Close()
175	
176		for rows.Next() {
177			var n trackLocationNote
178			var srcID sql.NullInt64
179			if rows.Scan(&n.Lat, &n.Lon, &n.Note, &srcID, &n.FeedbackScore) == nil {
180				if srcID.Valid {
181					n.SourceChatID = srcID.Int64
182				}
183				// Skip notes that mention a different track ID.
184				if noteTrack := extractTrackID(n.Note); noteTrack != "" && noteTrack != trackID {
185					continue
186				}
187				out = append(out, n)
188			}
189		}
190		return out
191	}
192

package main

var referenceData = map[string]string{
	"units": `# Radiation Units

**µSv/h (microsieverts per hour)**
- Most common unit for dose rate
- Measures biological impact of radiation exposure over time
- 1 µSv/h = 0.001 mSv/h = 0.000001 Sv/h

**CPM (Counts Per Minute)**
- Raw count of radiation events detected
- Device-specific, varies by detector sensitivity
- Typical conversion: ~100 CPM ≈ 1 µSv/h (varies by device)

**Bq (Becquerel)**
- Measures radioactivity of a source
- 1 Bq = 1 decay per second
- Used for contamination levels

**Sv (Sievert)**
- SI unit for radiation dose
- 1 Sv = 1000 mSv = 1,000,000 µSv
- Accounts for biological effect
`,

	"dose_rates": `# Understanding Dose Rates

**Typical Dose Rate Ranges:**

**0.05 - 0.20 µSv/h**
- Normal background radiation
- Varies by location (altitude, geology)

**0.20 - 0.50 µSv/h**
- Slightly elevated, still safe
- Common in granite-rich areas

**0.50 - 1.00 µSv/h**
- Elevated but not immediately dangerous
- Should investigate source

**1.00 - 10.00 µSv/h**
- Clearly elevated
- Avoid prolonged exposure
- May indicate contamination

**> 10.00 µSv/h**
- High radiation area
- Minimize exposure time
- Seek expert advice

## Issue #6 — What's Done, What's Easy, What's Hard

### Already Implemented

**1. MCP-Compatible API Layer** — Fully done. 18+ MCP tools registered (`query_radiation`, `search_area`, `get_track`, `get_spectrum`, `list_spectra`, `radiation_info`, `sensor_history`, `query_analytics`, `extreme_readings`, etc.). Both REST and MCP interfaces share the same backend.

**11. Spectrometry Comparison** — Largely done. `get_spectrum` and `list_spectra` tools exist, spectra data is served from DB, and the AI can compare them via chat.

**12. Isotope Interpretation** — Partially done. `radiation_info` tool includes reference data on isotopes (Cs-137, K-40, radon, building materials, etc.) and the LLM can reason over it.

**15. User-Friendly Final Response** — Done structurally. The web-chat interface + Claude as the AI layer already produce plain-language answers from tool results.

**10. High-Risk Point Integration** — Partially done. `query_extreme_readings` tool surfaces anomalous readings; the AI can correlate them.

---

### Fast/Simple to Add

**2. Automatic Post-Upload Analytics (baseline + anomaly flag)** — The DuckLake analytics tables and track insights endpoint already exist. Adding a post-upload trigger that computes a simple mean/stddev baseline and flags readings >3σ is a self-contained addition (~1-2 days).

**8. Water Contamination Context (basic)** — Could be done as a prompt-side enrichment: given a location, fetch upstream river data from an open API (e.g. OpenStreetMap's waterways or a river routing API) and pass it as context. No new DB schema needed — just a new MCP tool calling an external API (~1-2 days).

**6. Geological Assessment (basic)** — The reference_data.go already mentions granite/radon/geology. Adding a `geology_context` tool that calls a free API (e.g. Macrostrat or OpenTopography) for a lat/lon could be done quickly as a read-through MCP tool (~1 day).

**7. Technogenic Source Mapping (basic)** — Could add an MCP tool that queries OpenStreetMap Overpass API for nearby industrial/nuclear/mining points of interest given a bounding box. Straightforward tool addition (~1 day).

**13. User Scenario Collection (basic UI)** — A structured questionnaire in the web-chat (residence type, water source, food sourcing) as a pre-chat onboarding flow. JSON stored as chat context. Moderate UI + backend work but no new infrastructure (~2-3 days).

---

### Requires Significant Structural Work

**3. Full Contextual Data Integration** — Wikimapia, geological APIs, hydrological systems, fire databases, spectral libraries — each is a separate integration. Requires API key management, data normalization, caching strategy, and keeping data fresh. This is a multi-month effort if done properly.

**4. Weather/Seasonal Context** — Needs a weather API integration (e.g. Open-Meteo historical) tied to measurement timestamps + location. The tricky part is correlating *historical* weather with *past* measurements. Needs new DB fields and a backfill pipeline.

**5. Fire Risk Analysis** — Requires integrating real-time and historical fire perimeter data (FIRMS/NASA), atmospheric dispersion logic, and resuspension models. This is genuinely scientific/domain-expert work beyond just an API call.

**9. Atmospheric Transport / HYSPLIT** — HYSPLIT is a full atmospheric trajectory model from NOAA. Integrating it means either running the model locally (complex, large) or calling an external service. This is research-grade work — weeks to months.

**14. Exposure Pathway Assessment** — Separate modelling of gamma, radon, water, food chain, inhalation, residence duration — this requires dosimetry models, not just data lookups. Essentially a domain-specific scientific computing problem.

## Implementation Plan: Four New Features

### Codebase Architecture Summary (for reference)

Before detailing each feature, here are the invariants every new feature must respect:

1. New MCP tools go in `cmd/unified-server/tool_<name>.go` AND a copy in `cmd/mcp-server/tool_<name>.go`
2. Both `cmd/unified-server/mcp_register.go` and `cmd/mcp-server/main.go` need `mcpServer.AddTool(...)` lines
3. Use `instrumentMCP(...)` in unified-server and `instrument(...)` in mcp-server
4. Tool handlers use `jsonResult(map[string]any{...})` to return, `mcp.NewToolResultError(...)` for errors
5. External HTTP calls: `http.NewRequestWithContext` with a `context.WithTimeout` of 10–15 s; no new dependencies
6. DuckDB schema changes go in `createDuckDBSchema()` in `duckdb_analytics.go`
7. The `track_insights` endpoint is registered only in unified-server's `RegisterMCP()` on the main mux; the mcp-server binary has its own REST handler registration path via `rest.Register(mux)`

---

## Feature 2 — Post-Upload Anomaly Detection

### Goal

After a track upload completes, compute statistical anomalies (readings > 3σ above mean/median) asynchronously and store a compact summary in DuckLake. The existing `GET /api/track/{id}/insights` endpoint in `track_insights.go` must include this anomaly summary.

### Files to Create or Modify

| Action | File |
|--------|------|
| Create | `cmd/unified-server/track_anomaly.go` |
| Modify | `cmd/unified-server/duckdb_analytics.go` — add `track_anomaly_summaries` table DDL |
| Modify | `cmd/unified-server/main.go` — call `triggerAnomalyAnalysis(trackID)` at the upload completion point |
| Modify | `cmd/unified-server/track_insights.go` — add `AnomalySummary` field to response; call `loadAnomalySummary` |

### New DuckLake Table

Add to `createDuckDBSchema()` in `duckdb_analytics.go`:

```sql
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS track_anomaly_summaries (
    track_id    VARCHAR NOT NULL,
    computed_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT now(),
    reading_count   INTEGER,
    mean_doserate   DOUBLE,
    median_doserate DOUBLE,
    stddev_doserate DOUBLE,
    threshold       DOUBLE,     -- mean + 3*stddev
    anomaly_count   INTEGER,
    anomaly_ids     VARCHAR,    -- JSON array of marker IDs
    anomaly_max     DOUBLE,     -- highest anomalous reading
    anomaly_min     DOUBLE,     -- lowest anomalous reading
    PRIMARY KEY (track_id)
);
```

The `PRIMARY KEY` hint is important: DuckLake supports it and allows upsert-style recomputation on re-upload.

### New File: `cmd/unified-server/track_anomaly.go`

Key data structures and functions:

```go
type AnomalySummary struct {
    TrackID       string    `json:"track_id"`
    ComputedAt    time.Time `json:"computed_at"`
    ReadingCount  int       `json:"reading_count"`
    MeanDoserate  float64   `json:"mean_doserate"`
    MedianDoserate float64  `json:"median_doserate"`
    StddevDoserate float64  `json:"stddev_doserate"`
    Threshold     float64   `json:"threshold"`
    AnomalyCount  int       `json:"anomaly_count"`
    AnomalyIDs    []int64   `json:"anomaly_ids,omitempty"`
    AnomalyMax    float64   `json:"anomaly_max,omitempty"`
    AnomalyMin    float64   `json:"anomaly_min,omitempty"`
}

func triggerAnomalyAnalysis(trackID string)
func computeAnomalies(trackID string) (*AnomalySummary, error)
func storeAnomalySummary(s *AnomalySummary) error
func loadAnomalySummary(trackID string) (*AnomalySummary, error)
```

`triggerAnomalyAnalysis` launches a goroutine, waits 2 seconds (to let the database commit settle), then calls `computeAnomalies`.

`computeAnomalies` queries PostgreSQL via the shared `db.DB` connection:

```sql
SELECT id, doserate FROM markers WHERE trackid = $1 AND doserate > 0 AND doserate < 10000
```

It collects all `doserate` values, computes mean, stddev (Welford's single-pass algorithm to avoid two passes), and median (sort the slice). Any reading where `doserate > mean + 3*stddev` is flagged. The first 50 anomaly IDs are serialised as a JSON array and stored in `anomaly_ids`. If there are zero readings or stddev is 0, a "no anomalies" record is stored with `anomaly_count = 0`.

`storeAnomalySummary` inserts with a `DELETE + INSERT` pattern (DuckLake does not support `ON CONFLICT`):

```go
duckDB.Exec(`DELETE FROM track_anomaly_summaries WHERE track_id = ?`, s.TrackID)
duckDB.Exec(`INSERT INTO track_anomaly_summaries (...) VALUES (?, ?, ?, ...)`, ...)
```

`loadAnomalySummary` does a single `SELECT` from DuckLake and deserialises `anomaly_ids` from JSON.

### Integration Point: `main.go` Upload Completion

The background goroutine in `uploadHandler` currently sets `prog.Complete = true` and `prog.RedirectURL` (around line 4740). Immediately after setting `RedirectURL` and before `prog.mu.Unlock()`, add:

```go
if trackID != "" && !isSpectrumUpload && !backgroundImport {
    triggerAnomalyAnalysis(trackID)
}
```

This is safe: `triggerAnomalyAnalysis` launches its own goroutine so it does not block the upload completion path.

### Integration Point: `track_insights.go`

Extend `trackInsightsResponse` to add:

```go
AnomalySummary *AnomalySummary `json:"anomaly_summary,omitempty"`
```

In `trackInsightsHandler`, after loading `bounds`, call `loadAnomalySummary(trackID)` and attach the result (nil is omitted by `omitempty`).

### Order of Implementation

1. Add the DDL in `duckdb_analytics.go`
2. Create `track_anomaly.go` with all four functions
3. Wire `triggerAnomalyAnalysis` into `main.go` upload completion
4. Extend `trackInsightsResponse` and `trackInsightsHandler` in `track_insights.go`
5. Test with a fresh upload and then `GET /api/track/{id}/insights`

## Feature 6 — Geology Context MCP Tool

### Goal

New MCP tool `geology_context` that accepts `lat` and `lon`, calls the Macrostrat API to return geological unit, lithology, age, and estimated natural radiation background context.

### Files to Create or Modify

| Action | File |
|--------|------|
| Create | `cmd/unified-server/tool_geology_context.go` |
| Create | `cmd/mcp-server/tool_geology_context.go` (identical copy) |
| Modify | `cmd/unified-server/mcp_register.go` — add `AddTool` line |
| Modify | `cmd/mcp-server/main.go` — add `AddTool` line |

### Tool Definition Pattern

```go
var geologyContextToolDef = mcp.NewTool("geology_context",
    mcp.WithDescription("Get geological context for a location: rock type (lithology), geologic formation, age, and natural radiation background estimate. Useful for understanding whether elevated readings may relate to naturally radioactive geology (granites, black shales, uranium-bearing rocks) or radon potential. Calls the Macrostrat API."),
    mcp.WithNumber("lat",
        mcp.Description("Latitude (decimal degrees, -90 to 90)"),
        mcp.Min(-90), mcp.Max(90),
        mcp.Required(),
    ),
    mcp.WithNumber("lon",
        mcp.Description("Longitude (decimal degrees, -180 to 180)"),
        mcp.Min(-180), mcp.Max(180),
        mcp.Required(),
    ),
    mcp.WithReadOnlyHintAnnotation(true),
)
```

### Macrostrat API Call

Macrostrat provides free, unauthenticated access. The relevant endpoint is:

```
GET https://macrostrat.org/api/v2/units?lat={lat}&lng={lon}&response=long&format=json
```

This returns geologic columns with `lith` (lithology), `strat_name`, `t_age` (top age), `b_age` (bottom age), `environ` (environment), and `color`. A second call to `https://macrostrat.org/api/v2/columns?lat={lat}&lng={lon}&format=json` provides the column-level identifier.

The handler should:

1. Build the URL with `fmt.Sprintf`
2. Use `http.NewRequestWithContext` with a 12-second timeout
3. Parse the JSON response into a struct sufficient to extract the top result
4. Derive a qualitative `radiation_background` hint from lithology keywords:
   - "granite", "granodiorite", "rhyolite" → "elevated (felsic igneous, K-U-Th enriched)"
   - "shale", "black shale", "carbonaceous" → "potentially elevated (organic-rich shales)"
   - "limestone", "sandstone", "basalt" → "typical background"
   - "evaporite", "salt" → "low"
   - Default → "unknown"
5. Return structured text with all fields plus the derived hint

No new Go structs need to be exported; inner anonymous structs used for JSON decode are fine. The result returned to the LLM should be a `map[string]any` following the `jsonResult` pattern. Always include `_ai_hint` with the standard formatting directive.

### Error Handling

If Macrostrat returns no units (outside coverage), return a `"coverage": false` result rather than an error, so the LLM can still report "no geological data available for this location."

### mcp-server copy

The file in `cmd/mcp-server/tool_geology_context.go` is byte-for-byte identical except the `package main` declaration stays `package main` (both are package main). There are no imports that differ between the two binaries; both use standard library only.

### Registration

In `cmd/unified-server/mcp_register.go`, after the `searchTracksLocationToolDef` line:
```go
mcpServer.AddTool(geologyContextToolDef, instrumentMCP("geology_context", handleGeologyContext))
```

In `cmd/mcp-server/main.go`, after the `searchTracksLocationToolDef` line:
```go
mcpServer.AddTool(geologyContextToolDef, instrument("geology_context", handleGeologyContext))
```

### Order of Implementation

1. Create `tool_geology_context.go` in unified-server
2. Copy to mcp-server
3. Add `AddTool` in both registration files
4. Manual test: `curl` the tool with known granite coordinates (e.g., New Hampshire, USA: 43.5, -71.5) and with ocean coordinates to test the no-coverage path

---

## Feature 7 — Technogenic POI Lookup via Overpass API

### Goal

New MCP tool `technogenic_pois` that accepts `lat`, `lon`, and `radius_km`, queries the OSM Overpass API for nearby industrial/nuclear/mining/waste POIs, and returns a ranked list of POIs with name, type, and distance.

### Files to Create or Modify

| Action | File |
|--------|------|
| Create | `cmd/unified-server/tool_technogenic_pois.go` |
| Create | `cmd/mcp-server/tool_technogenic_pois.go` |
| Modify | `cmd/unified-server/mcp_register.go` |
| Modify | `cmd/mcp-server/main.go` |

### Tool Definition

```go
var technogenicPOIsToolDef = mcp.NewTool("technogenic_pois",
    mcp.WithDescription("Find nearby industrial, nuclear, and mining points of interest from OpenStreetMap. Returns power plants, mines, quarries, waste sites, tailings ponds, and similar technogenic radiation sources within a given radius. Useful for understanding potential anthropogenic sources of elevated radiation readings."),
    mcp.WithNumber("lat",
        mcp.Description("Center latitude"),
        mcp.Min(-90), mcp.Max(90),
        mcp.Required(),
    ),
    mcp.WithNumber("lon",
        mcp.Description("Center longitude"),
        mcp.Min(-180), mcp.Max(180),
        mcp.Required(),
    ),
    mcp.WithNumber("radius_km",
        mcp.Description("Search radius in kilometres (0.5 to 100)"),
        mcp.Min(0.5), mcp.Max(100),
        mcp.DefaultNumber(25),
    ),
    mcp.WithReadOnlyHintAnnotation(true),
)
```

### Overpass QL Query

The Overpass API accepts QL queries via `POST https://overpass-api.de/api/interpreter` with `data=<query>`. The query body should use `[out:json][timeout:25]` and target the following OSM tags:

```
[out:json][timeout:25];
(
  node["power"="plant"](around:{radius_m},{lat},{lon});
  way["power"="plant"](around:{radius_m},{lat},{lon});
  node["landuse"="industrial"](around:{radius_m},{lat},{lon});
  way["landuse"="industrial"](around:{radius_m},{lat},{lon});
  node["landuse"="quarry"](around:{radius_m},{lat},{lon});
  way["landuse"="quarry"](around:{radius_m},{lat},{lon});
  node["landuse"="landfill"](around:{radius_m},{lat},{lon});
  way["landuse"="landfill"](around:{radius_m},{lat},{lon});
  node["man_made"="tailings_pond"](around:{radius_m},{lat},{lon});
  way["man_made"="tailings_pond"](around:{radius_m},{lat},{lon});
  node["man_made"="mine"](around:{radius_m},{lat},{lon});
  way["man_made"="mine"](around:{radius_m},{lat},{lon});
  node["industrial"~"nuclear|uranium|chemical|waste"](around:{radius_m},{lat},{lon});
  way["industrial"~"nuclear|uranium|chemical|waste"](around:{radius_m},{lat},{lon});
);
out center tags;
```

The `out center` modifier returns the centroid of way geometries, making distance calculation uniform.

### Distance Calculation

After fetching results, compute Haversine distance from `(lat, lon)` to each POI's centroid (`element.lat` for nodes, `element.center.lat` for ways). Sort by distance ascending. Cap the returned list at 50 POIs.

```go
func haversineKm(lat1, lon1, lat2, lon2 float64) float64 {
    const R = 6371.0
    dLat := (lat2 - lat1) * math.Pi / 180
    dLon := (lon2 - lon1) * math.Pi / 180
    a := math.Sin(dLat/2)*math.Sin(dLat/2) +
        math.Cos(lat1*math.Pi/180)*math.Cos(lat2*math.Pi/180)*
            math.Sin(dLon/2)*math.Sin(dLon/2)
    return R * 2 * math.Atan2(math.Sqrt(a), math.Sqrt(1-a))
}
```

### POI Struct

```go
type poiResult struct {
    Name       string  `json:"name"`
    Type       string  `json:"type"`       // "power_plant", "quarry", "mine", etc.
    Tags       map[string]string `json:"tags,omitempty"`
    Lat        float64 `json:"lat"`
    Lon        float64 `json:"lon"`
    DistanceKm float64 `json:"distance_km"`
}
```

The `type` field is derived from the most specific OSM tag: `power=plant` → "power_plant"; `landuse=quarry` → "quarry"; `man_made=tailings_pond` → "tailings_pond"; `industrial` value used directly; `landuse=landfill` → "landfill". A helper `classifyPOI(tags map[string]string) string` encodes this logic.

### HTTP Request Pattern

Use `strings.NewReader("data=" + url.QueryEscape(query))` as the request body with `Content-Type: application/x-www-form-urlencoded`. Set timeout to 15 seconds. If Overpass returns HTTP 429 (too many requests), return a graceful error message to the LLM rather than a Go error.

### Registration (same pattern as Feature 6)

### Order of Implementation

1. Create `tool_technogenic_pois.go` with the QL template, Overpass fetch, Haversine, and sort
2. Copy to mcp-server
3. Register in both files
4. Test with coordinates near a known nuclear facility (e.g., Fukushima Daiichi: 37.42, 141.03) and near a rural area

---

## Feature 8 — Water Route Context MCP Tool

### Goal

New MCP tool `water_context` that accepts `lat` and `lon`, queries the OSM Overpass API for nearby waterways (rivers, streams, canals, lakes) and coastlines, and returns a structured description useful for understanding water contamination transport pathways.

### Files to Create or Modify

| Action | File |
|--------|------|
| Create | `cmd/unified-server/tool_water_context.go` |
| Create | `cmd/mcp-server/tool_water_context.go` |
| Modify | `cmd/unified-server/mcp_register.go` |
| Modify | `cmd/mcp-server/main.go` |

### Tool Definition

```go
var waterContextToolDef = mcp.NewTool("water_context",
    mcp.WithDescription("Get nearby waterways and water bodies from OpenStreetMap for a given location. Returns rivers, streams, canals, lakes, reservoirs, and coastlines within a search radius. Useful for understanding how radioactive contamination might be transported via waterways — for example, runoff from contaminated soil into rivers, or dilution in water bodies. Use this alongside radiation readings near water."),
    mcp.WithNumber("lat",
        mcp.Description("Latitude"),
        mcp.Min(-90), mcp.Max(90),
        mcp.Required(),
    ),
    mcp.WithNumber("lon",
        mcp.Description("Longitude"),
        mcp.Min(-180), mcp.Max(180),
        mcp.Required(),
    ),
    mcp.WithNumber("radius_km",
        mcp.Description("Search radius in kilometres (0.5 to 50)"),
        mcp.Min(0.5), mcp.Max(50),
        mcp.DefaultNumber(10),
    ),
    mcp.WithReadOnlyHintAnnotation(true),
)
```

### Overpass QL Query

```
[out:json][timeout:25];
(
  way["waterway"~"river|stream|canal|drain|ditch"](around:{radius_m},{lat},{lon});
  node["waterway"~"river|stream|canal"](around:{radius_m},{lat},{lon});
  way["natural"="water"](around:{radius_m},{lat},{lon});
  relation["natural"="water"](around:{radius_m},{lat},{lon});
  way["natural"="coastline"](around:{radius_m},{lat},{lon});
  node["natural"="spring"](around:{radius_m},{lat},{lon});
  way["landuse"="reservoir"](around:{radius_m},{lat},{lon});
);
out center tags;
```

### Response Structure

The handler should aggregate results into categories and return:

```json
{
  "location": {"lat": ..., "lon": ...},
  "radius_km": ...,
  "waterways": [
    {"name": "Abukuma River", "type": "river", "distance_km": 1.2},
    ...
  ],
  "water_bodies": [
    {"name": "Lake Fukushima", "type": "reservoir", "distance_km": 3.5},
    ...
  ],
  "coastal": false,
  "nearest_waterway": {"name": "...", "type": "...", "distance_km": ...},
  "context_summary": "Location is 1.2 km from the Abukuma River (river). 2 water bodies within 10 km radius. No coastal proximity.",
  "count": 5,
  "_ai_hint": "..."
}
```

The `context_summary` string is synthesised in Go (not by the LLM) from the structured data. This gives the LLM a compact natural-language paragraph to integrate into its response without having to iterate the full list.

The `coastal` flag is `true` if any element has `natural=coastline`. `nearest_waterway` is the closest element with `waterway` tag present.

The same `haversineKm` function from Feature 7 is used. To avoid duplication, this function should be placed in a shared file.

### Shared Helper

Rather than duplicating `haversineKm` in both Feature 7 and Feature 8, define it once in a new file `cmd/unified-server/geo_helpers.go` (and copy to `cmd/mcp-server/geo_helpers.go`):

```go
// geo_helpers.go — shared geographic utilities for MCP tools
package main

import "math"

// haversineKm returns the great-circle distance in kilometres between two points.
func haversineKm(lat1, lon1, lat2, lon2 float64) float64 { ... }
```

Both `tool_technogenic_pois.go` and `tool_water_context.go` then call `haversineKm` without re-defining it.

### Registration (same pattern)

### Order of Implementation

1. Create `cmd/unified-server/geo_helpers.go` with `haversineKm`
2. Create `tool_water_context.go`
3. Copy both new files to mcp-server
4. Register in both registration files
5. Test with Fukushima coordinates (near Abukuma River) and a landlocked dry region

## Global Implementation Order (Across All Four Features)

This sequence minimises merge conflicts and ensures each step is independently buildable:

1. **Schema first** — Add `track_anomaly_summaries` DDL in `duckdb_analytics.go`. Run the server locally to confirm `createDuckDBSchema()` succeeds.

2. **Shared geo helper** — Create `geo_helpers.go` in both binaries. This is a pure utility with no dependencies and can be verified by `go build`.

3. **Feature 6 (geology_context)** — Smallest external API integration; good to do early to establish the Macrostrat API behaviour pattern. Create tool files, register, test.

4. **Feature 7 (technogenic_pois)** — Similar Overpass integration. Reuses `haversineKm`. Create tool files, register, test.

5. **Feature 8 (water_context)** — Same Overpass pattern, slightly different query. Create tool files, register, test.

6. **Feature 2 (anomaly detection)** — Most complex; touches `main.go` (the largest file), `track_insights.go`, and DuckLake. Do last so the schema additions (step 1) and the insights response extension are already stable. Create `track_anomaly.go`, wire into upload handler, extend `track_insights.go`.

---

## Potential Challenges and Mitigations

**DuckLake `PRIMARY KEY` support**: DuckLake's Parquet-backed tables may not enforce PK constraints like PostgreSQL does. The `DELETE + INSERT` pattern for `storeAnomalySummary` is safer than `INSERT OR REPLACE`. Test this during step 1.

**Overpass API rate limiting**: The Overpass API returns HTTP 429 when overloaded. Both Feature 7 and 8 handlers must check for this and return a `mcp.NewToolResultError` message that tells the LLM "Overpass API is temporarily unavailable, try again in a few minutes." Do not retry inside the handler.

**Macrostrat coverage gaps**: The Macrostrat API covers primarily North America and some global datasets. For locations outside coverage it returns an empty `success.data` array. The handler must handle `len(units) == 0` gracefully.

**Upload handler goroutine capture**: Inside the upload goroutine in `main.go`, `trackID` is a variable that gets reassigned per file (e.g. `bbox, trackID, err = processCSVFile(...)`). The call to `triggerAnomalyAnalysis(trackID)` must happen after the entire per-file loop completes and use the final `trackID` value, not a closure-captured pre-loop value. The correct insertion point is after the loop ends (after line 4719) and before the `uploadProgress.Lock()` at line 4742.

**`math` import in geo helpers**: The unified-server `main.go` already imports `"math"`. The `geo_helpers.go` file will add its own import, which is fine since they are separate files in the same package.

**DuckDB single-connection constraint**: `duckDB.SetMaxOpenConns(1)` means DuckLake writes from `storeAnomalySummary` and reads from `loadAnomalySummary` must handle lock contention. The `triggerAnomalyAnalysis` goroutine includes the 2-second delay before writing, and `loadAnomalySummary` uses a fresh `SELECT` query, so they will not deadlock. But if DuckLake is under heavy write load (multiple concurrent uploads), the goroutine may wait. This is acceptable since anomaly analysis is best-effort, not user-facing in real time.

---

## Implementation Plan: 4 New Features

### Global Implementation Order

1. Schema DDL (`duckdb_analytics.go`)
2. Shared geo helper (`geo_helpers.go`)

4. Feature 7 — technogenic POI lookup

### Feature 2 — Post-Upload Anomaly Detection

**New file:** `cmd/unified-server/track_anomaly.go`

**New DuckLake table** (add to `createDuckDBSchema()` in [duckdb_analytics.go](cmd/unified-server/duckdb_analytics.go)):
```sql
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS track_anomaly_summaries (
    track_id VARCHAR, computed_at TIMESTAMPTZ, reading_count INTEGER,
    mean_doserate DOUBLE, median_doserate DOUBLE, stddev_doserate DOUBLE,
    threshold DOUBLE, anomaly_count INTEGER,
    anomaly_ids VARCHAR, anomaly_max DOUBLE, anomaly_min DOUBLE
);
```

**Functions to implement in `track_anomaly.go`:**
- `triggerAnomalyAnalysis(trackID)` — launches a goroutine, waits 2s, then calls `computeAnomalies`
- `computeAnomalies(trackID)` — queries PostgreSQL `markers` for all doserate values, computes mean+stddev (Welford single-pass), flags readings > mean+3σ
- `storeAnomalySummary(s)` — `DELETE + INSERT` into DuckLake (no `ON CONFLICT` support)
- `loadAnomalySummary(trackID)` — `SELECT` from DuckLake for the insights endpoint

**Hook into [main.go](cmd/unified-server/main.go):** After the upload processing loop finishes and before `uploadProgress.Lock()`, call `triggerAnomalyAnalysis(trackID)`.

**Extend [track_insights.go](cmd/unified-server/track_insights.go):** Add `AnomalySummary *AnomalySummary \`json:"anomaly_summary,omitempty"\`` to `trackInsightsResponse` and call `loadAnomalySummary` in the handler.

---

### Feature 6 — Geology Context Tool

**New files:** `cmd/unified-server/tool_geology_context.go`, `cmd/mcp-server/tool_geology_context.go`

Tool name: `geology_context`, params: `lat`, `lon`

Calls **Macrostrat API**: `GET https://macrostrat.org/api/v2/units?lat={lat}&lng={lon}&response=long&format=json`

Derives a qualitative `radiation_background` hint from lithology keywords:
- `granite/granodiorite/rhyolite` → "elevated (felsic igneous, K-U-Th enriched)"
- `shale/black shale` → "potentially elevated"
- `limestone/sandstone/basalt` → "typical background"
- no coverage → return `"coverage": false` (not an error)

**Register** in [mcp_register.go](cmd/unified-server/mcp_register.go) and [cmd/mcp-server/main.go](cmd/mcp-server/main.go).

---

### Feature 7 — Technogenic POI Lookup (OSM)

**New files:** `cmd/unified-server/tool_technogenic_pois.go`, `cmd/mcp-server/tool_technogenic_pois.go`

Tool name: `technogenic_pois`, params: `lat`, `lon`, `radius_km` (default 25, max 100)

Calls **Overpass API** via `POST https://overpass-api.de/api/interpreter` with QL query targeting: `power=plant`, `landuse=industrial/quarry/landfill`, `man_made=tailings_pond/mine`, `industrial~nuclear|uranium|chemical|waste`

Returns sorted list of up to 50 POIs with name, type, distance (Haversine). Handle HTTP 429 gracefully (return LLM-readable message, don't retry).

Uses `haversineKm` from shared `geo_helpers.go`.

---

### Feature 8 — Water Route Context

**New files:** `cmd/unified-server/tool_water_context.go`, `cmd/mcp-server/tool_water_context.go`

Tool name: `water_context`, params: `lat`, `lon`, `radius_km` (default 10, max 50)

Same Overpass pattern, targeting: `waterway~river|stream|canal|drain|ditch`, `natural=water/coastline/spring`, `landuse=reservoir`

Returns structured result with `waterways[]`, `water_bodies[]`, `coastal` bool, `nearest_waterway`, and a pre-computed `context_summary` string (synthesised in Go, not by the LLM).

---

### Shared Helper

**New file:** `cmd/unified-server/geo_helpers.go` (+ copy to `cmd/mcp-server/`)

Contains `haversineKm(lat1, lon1, lat2, lon2 float64) float64` — used by both Feature 7 and Feature 8.

---

### Key Risks to Watch

| Risk | Mitigation |
|------|-----------|
| DuckLake no `ON CONFLICT` | Use `DELETE + INSERT` in `storeAnomalySummary` |
| Overpass rate limiting (HTTP 429) | Return graceful LLM error, no retry |
| Macrostrat coverage gaps | Handle empty `data[]` as `coverage: false` |
| Upload goroutine variable capture | Call `triggerAnomalyAnalysis` after the per-file loop, not inside it |
| DuckDB single-connection contention | Anomaly analysis is best-effort; the 2s delay is sufficient for typical loads |

1	// DuckDB Analytics Initialization for Unified Server
2	// Uses DuckLake with PostgreSQL catalog for shared analytics across all services
3	
4	package main
5	
6	import (
7		"database/sql"
8		"fmt"
9		"log"
10		"os"
11		"strings"
12	
13		_ "github.com/duckdb/duckdb-go/v2"
14	)
15	
16	var duckDB *sql.DB
17	
18	// initDuckDBAnalytics initializes DuckDB with DuckLake catalog backed by PostgreSQL.
19	// This allows multiple services to share the same analytics tables concurrently.
20	func initDuckDBAnalytics() error {
21		// Open in-memory DuckDB — all persistent data lives in DuckLake (PostgreSQL + Parquet)
22		var err error
23		duckDB, err = sql.Open("duckdb", "")
24		if err != nil {
25			return fmt.Errorf("failed to open duckdb: %w", err)
26		}
27	
28		duckDB.SetMaxOpenConns(1)
29		duckDB.SetMaxIdleConns(1)
30		duckDB.SetConnMaxLifetime(0)
31	
32		if err := duckDB.Ping(); err != nil {
33			return fmt.Errorf("failed to ping duckdb: %w", err)
34		}
35	
36		log.Println("DuckDB initialized (in-memory)")
37	
38		// Install and load required extensions
39		for _, ext := range []string{"ducklake", "postgres"} {
40			if _, err := duckDB.Exec(fmt.Sprintf("INSTALL %s;", ext)); err != nil {
41				log.Printf("Warning: INSTALL %s failed: %v", ext, err)
42			}
43			if _, err := duckDB.Exec(fmt.Sprintf("LOAD %s;", ext)); err != nil {
44				return fmt.Errorf("LOAD %s: %w", ext, err)
45			}
46		}
47	
48		// Attach DuckLake catalog via PostgreSQL (optional — falls back to in-memory for local dev)
49		ducklakePGURL := os.Getenv("DUCKLAKE_PG_URL")
50		if ducklakePGURL == "" {
51			ducklakePGURL = "dbname=ducklake_catalog host=localhost user=ducklake_rw"
52		}
53		dataPath := os.Getenv("DUCKLAKE_DATA_PATH")
54		if dataPath == "" {
55			dataPath = "/var/lib/safecast/ducklake/"
56		}
57	
58		attachQuery := fmt.Sprintf(
59			"ATTACH 'ducklake:postgres:%s' AS analytics (DATA_PATH '%s');",
60			ducklakePGURL, dataPath,
61		)
62		ducklakeOK := false
63		if _, err := duckDB.Exec(attachQuery); err != nil {
64			log.Printf("Warning: DuckLake attach failed (%v) — falling back to in-memory tables (local dev mode)", err)
65		} else {
66			log.Printf("DuckLake attached (catalog=PostgreSQL, data=%s)", dataPath)
67			if _, err := duckDB.Exec("USE analytics;"); err != nil {
68				log.Printf("Warning: USE analytics failed: %v — using in-memory tables", err)
69			} else {
70				ducklakeOK = true
71			}
72		}
73		if !ducklakeOK {
74			log.Println("DuckDB running in-memory only (analytics will not persist across restarts)")
75		}
76	
77		// Also attach main Safecast PostgreSQL for cross-database queries (read-only)
78		databaseURL := os.Getenv("DATABASE_URL")
79		if databaseURL == "" {
80			// Unified server uses -db-conn flag; construct URL from that
81			// Fall back to environment variable
82		}
83		if databaseURL != "" {
84			if err := attachPostgres(databaseURL); err != nil {
85				log.Printf("Warning: PostgreSQL attach failed: %v (cross-db analytics disabled)", err)
86			} else {
87				log.Println("Safecast PostgreSQL attached for cross-database queries")
88			}
89		}
90	
91		// Create analytics schema in DuckLake
92		if err := createDuckDBSchema(); err != nil {
93			log.Printf("Warning: failed to create DuckLake schema: %v", err)
94		}
95	
96		return nil
97	}
98	
99	// attachPostgres attaches the main Safecast PostgreSQL for read-only cross-database queries
100	func attachPostgres(databaseURL string) error {
101		attachStr := databaseURL
102		if !strings.Contains(databaseURL, "?") {
103			attachStr = databaseURL + "?sslmode=prefer"
104		}
105	
106		query := fmt.Sprintf("ATTACH '%s' AS postgres_db (TYPE POSTGRES, READ_ONLY);", attachStr)
107		if _, err := duckDB.Exec(query); err != nil {
108			return fmt.Errorf("attach postgres: %w", err)
109		}
110		return nil
111	}
112	
113	// createDuckDBSchema creates the shared analytics tables in DuckLake
114	func createDuckDBSchema() error {
115		// DuckLake doesn't support multi-statement exec, so run each separately
116		tables := []string{
117			`CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS mcp_query_log (
118				tool_name VARCHAR,
119				params JSON,
120				result_count INTEGER,
121				duration_ms DOUBLE,
122				client_info VARCHAR,
123				created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT now()
124			)`,
125			`CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS mcp_ai_query_log (
126				user_id VARCHAR,
127				user_email VARCHAR,
128				session_id VARCHAR,
129				timestamp TIMESTAMP,
130				tool_name VARCHAR,
131				generated_query VARCHAR,
132				duration_ms BIGINT,
133				commit_hash VARCHAR,
134				error VARCHAR
135			)`,
136			`CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS chat_feedback (
137				chat_id    BIGINT,
138				score      INTEGER,
139				created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT now()
140			)`,
141			`CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS chat_questions (
142				id BIGINT,
143				timestamp TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT now(),
144				question VARCHAR,
145				source VARCHAR,
146				ip_address VARCHAR,
147				user_agent VARCHAR,
148				is_mobile BOOLEAN,
149				os VARCHAR,
150				browser VARCHAR,
151				country VARCHAR,
152				accept_language VARCHAR,
153				referer VARCHAR,
154				session_id VARCHAR,
155				history_length INTEGER,
156				model VARCHAR,
157				cloudfront BOOLEAN,
158				client_timestamp TIMESTAMPTZ,
159				answer VARCHAR
160			)`,
161			// Semantic cache: stores embeddings + user feedback for past Q&A pairs.
162			// embedding is stored as a JSON array of float32 values (VARCHAR).
163			`CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS qa_embeddings (
164				id BIGINT,
165				chat_id BIGINT,
166				question VARCHAR,
167				answer VARCHAR,
168				embedding VARCHAR,
169				feedback_score INTEGER DEFAULT 0,
170				created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT now()
171			)`,
172			// Curated geographic knowledge: confirmed explanations for elevated/unusual
173			// readings at specific locations, auto-populated from positively-rated answers.
174			`CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS location_knowledge (
175				id BIGINT,
176				lat DOUBLE,
177				lon DOUBLE,
178				radius_m DOUBLE,
179				note VARCHAR,
180				source_chat_id BIGINT,
181				created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT now()
182			)`,
183		}
184	
185		for _, ddl := range tables {
186			if _, err := duckDB.Exec(ddl); err != nil {
187				return fmt.Errorf("create schema: %w", err)
188			}

192		return nil
193	}

17		mcp.WithDescription("Get aggregate radiation statistics from the Safecast database (e.g., average dose rate by year/month). Powered by DuckDB+Postgres."),
18		mcp.WithString("interval",
19			mcp.Description("Aggregation interval: 'year', 'month', or 'overall'"),
20			mcp.Enum("year", "month", "overall"),
21			mcp.DefaultString("year"),

27	func handleQueryAnalytics(ctx context.Context, req mcp.CallToolRequest) (*mcp.CallToolResult, error) {
28		if duckDB == nil {
29			return mcp.NewToolResultError("DuckDB analytics engine is not initialized"), nil
30		}
31	
32		// Execute query
33		rows, err := duckDB.Query(`
34			SELECT tool_name, COUNT(*) as count,
35	               AVG(duration_ms) as avg_ms,
36	               MAX(duration_ms) as max_ms
37			FROM mcp_query_log
38			GROUP BY tool_name
39			ORDER BY count DESC
40		`)
41		if err != nil {
42			return mcp.NewToolResultError(fmt.Sprintf("Query failed: %v", err)), nil
43		}
44		defer rows.Close()
45	
46		var stats []map[string]any
47		for rows.Next() {
48			var tool string
49			var count int64
50			var avgMs, maxMs float64
51			if err := rows.Scan(&tool, &count, &avgMs, &maxMs); err != nil {
52				continue
53			}
54			stats = append(stats, map[string]any{
55				"tool":   tool,
56				"calls":  count,
57				"avg_ms": avgMs,
58				"max_ms": maxMs,
59			})
60		}

```bash

The `query_analytics` tool runs a hardcoded stats query, not arbitrary SQL. The simpler test is to query DuckDB directly via psql on the DuckLake catalog:

1	// track_insights.go — GET /api/track/{id}/insights
2	//
3	// Returns cached RAG responses (Q&A pairs with positive feedback) that are
4	// spatially or semantically relevant to the given track, plus curated
5	// location_knowledge notes that fall within the track's bounding box.
6	//
7	// Relevance ranking:
8	//  1. Hard filter: only qa_embeddings with feedback_score > 0.
9	//  2. Semantic similarity: embed "radiation measurements near <centroid>"
10	//     and cosine-compare against stored embeddings (threshold = ragContextThreshold).
11	//  3. Return top 5 by similarity score.
12	//  4. Location notes: location_knowledge rows whose (lat, lon) falls inside bbox.
13	//
14	// Registration: http.HandleFunc("GET /api/track/{id}/insights", trackInsightsHandler)
15	// in mcp_register.go, registered on http.DefaultServeMux (port 8765).
16	
17	package main
18	
19	import (
20		"context"
21		"database/sql"
22		"encoding/json"
23		"fmt"
24		"net/http"
25		"sort"
26	
27		"safecast-new-map/pkg/database"
28	)
29	
30	// trackInsight is a single cached Q&A relevant to the track.
31	type trackInsight struct {
32		ChatID          int64   `json:"chat_id"`
33		Question        string  `json:"question"`
34		Answer          string  `json:"answer"`
35		SimilarityScore float32 `json:"similarity_score"`
36		FeedbackScore   int     `json:"feedback_score"`
37	}
38	
39	// trackLocationNote is a curated geographic note near the track.
40	type trackLocationNote struct {
41		Lat           float64 `json:"lat"`
42		Lon           float64 `json:"lon"`
43		Note          string  `json:"note"`
44		SourceChatID  int64   `json:"source_chat_id,omitempty"`
45		FeedbackScore int     `json:"feedback_score"`
46	}
47	
48	// trackInsightsResponse is the JSON payload for GET /api/track/{id}/insights.
49	type trackInsightsResponse struct {
50		TrackID       string              `json:"track_id"`
51		Insights      []trackInsight      `json:"insights"`
52		LocationNotes []trackLocationNote `json:"location_notes"`
53	}
54

{
  "file_path": "/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/cmd/unified-server/track_anomaly.go",
  "content": "// track_anomaly.go — post-upload anomaly detection for radiation tracks.\n//\n// After a track upload completes, triggerAnomalyAnalysis launches a background\n// goroutine that:\n//  1. Loads all doserate values for the track from PostgreSQL.\n//  2. Computes mean, median, and stddev (Welford single-pass).\n//  3. Flags readings where doserate > mean + 3*stddev.\n//  4. Stores the summary in the DuckLake track_anomaly_summaries table.\n//\n// The summary is exposed via GET /api/track/{id}/insights (track_insights.go).\n\npackage main\n\nimport (\n\t\"encoding/json\"\n\t\"fmt\"\n\t\"log\"\n\t\"math\"\n\t\"sort\"\n\t\"time\"\n)\n\n// AnomalySummary holds the statistical anomaly analysis for a single track.\ntype AnomalySummary struct {\n\tTrackID        string    `json:\"track_id\"`\n\tComputedAt     time.Time `json:\"computed_at\"`\n\tReadingCount   int       `json:\"reading_count\"`\n\tMeanDoserate   float64   `json:\"mean_doserate\"`\n\tMedianDoserate float64   `json:\"median_doserate\"`\n\tStddevDoserate float64   `json:\"stddev_doserate\"`\n\tThreshold      float64   `json:\"threshold\"`\n\tAnomalyCount   int       `json:\"anomaly_count\"`\n\tAnomalyIDs     []int64   `json:\"anomaly_ids,omitempty\"`\n\tAnomalyMax     float64   `json:\"anomaly_max,omitempty\"`\n\tAnomalyMin     float64   `json:\"anomaly_min,omitempty\"`\n}\n\n// triggerAnomalyAnalysis launches a background goroutine to analyse the track.\n// It waits 2 seconds to let the database commit settle before querying.\nfunc triggerAnomalyAnalysis(trackID string) {\n\tif trackID == \"\" {\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\tgo func() {\n\t\ttime.Sleep(2 * time.Second)\n\t\tsummary, err := computeAnomalies(trackID)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\tlog.Printf(\"[anomaly] %s: compute failed: %v\", trackID, err)\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\t\tif err := storeAnomalySummary(summary); err != nil {\n\t\t\tlog.Printf(\"[anomaly] %s: store failed: %v\", trackID, err)\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\t\tlog.Printf(\"[anomaly] %s: %d readings, %d anomalies (threshold %.4f µSv/h)\",\n\t\t\ttrackID, summary.ReadingCount, summary.AnomalyCount, summary.Threshold)\n\t}()\n}\n\n// computeAnomalies queries PostgreSQL for all doserate values on the track and\n// computes the statistical summary.\nfunc computeAnomalies(trackID string) (*AnomalySummary, error) {\n\tif db == nil {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"database not available\")\n\t}\n\n\trows, err := db.DB.Query(\n\t\t`SELECT id, doserate FROM markers\n\t\t WHERE trackid = $1 AND doserate > 0 AND doserate < 10000\n\t\t ORDER BY id`,\n\t\ttrackID,\n\t)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"query markers: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\tdefer rows.Close()\n\n\tvar ids []int64\n\tvar values []float64\n\tfor rows.Next() {\n\t\tvar id int64\n\t\tvar v float64\n\t\tif err := rows.Scan(&id, &v); err != nil {\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\tids = append(ids, id)\n\t\tvalues = append(values, v)\n\t}\n\n\tsummary := &AnomalySummary{\n\t\tTrackID:    trackID,\n\t\tComputedAt: time.Now().UTC(),\n\t}\n\n\tn := len(values)\n\tsummary.ReadingCount = n\n\n\tif n == 0 {\n\t\treturn summary, nil\n\t}\n\n\t// Mean and variance via Welford's online algorithm.\n\tvar mean, m2 float64\n\tfor i, v := range values {\n\t\tdelta := v - mean\n\t\tmean += delta / float64(i+1)\n\t\tm2 += delta * (v - mean)\n\t}\n\tstddev := 0.0\n\tif n > 1 {\n\t\tstddev = math.Sqrt(m2 / float64(n-1))\n\t}\n\n\t// Median via sorted copy.\n\tsorted := make([]float64, n)\n\tcopy(sorted, values)\n\tsort.Float64s(sorted)\n\tvar median float64\n\tif n%2 == 0 {\n\t\tmedian = (sorted[n/2-1] + sorted[n/2]) / 2\n\t} else {\n\t\tmedian = sorted[n/2]\n\t}\n\n\tthreshold := mean + 3*stddev\n\n\tsummary.MeanDoserate = mean\n\tsummary.MedianDoserate = median\n\tsummary.StddevDoserate = stddev\n\tsummary.Threshold = threshold\n\n\t// Flag anomalous readings.\n\tvar anomalyIDs []int64\n\tanomalyMax := -math.MaxFloat64\n\tanomalyMin := math.MaxFloat64\n\n\tfor i, v := range values {\n\t\tif v > threshold {\n\t\t\tanomalyIDs = append(anomalyIDs, ids[i])\n\t\t\tif v > anomalyMax {\n\t\t\t\tanomalyMax = v\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tif v < anomalyMin {\n\t\t\t\tanomalyMin = v\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\t// Cap stored IDs at 50 to keep the JSON compact.\n\tif len(anomalyIDs) > 50 {\n\t\tanomalyIDs = anomalyIDs[:50]\n\t}\n\n\tsummary.AnomalyCount = len(anomalyIDs)\n\tsummary.AnomalyIDs = anomalyIDs\n\tif summary.AnomalyCount > 0 {\n\t\tsummary.AnomalyMax = anomalyMax\n\t\tsummary.AnomalyMin = anomalyMin\n\t}\n\n\treturn summary, nil\n}\n\n// storeAnomalySummary persists the summary to DuckLake using DELETE + INSERT\n// (DuckLake does not support ON CONFLICT / upsert).\nfunc storeAnomalySummary(s *AnomalySummary) error {\n\tif !duckDBAvailable() {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"duckdb not available\")\n\t}\n\n\tidsJSON, err := json.Marshal(s.AnomalyIDs)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tidsJSON = []byte(\"[]\")\n\t}\n\n\tif _, err := duckDB.Exec(\n\t\t`DELETE FROM track_anomaly_summaries WHERE track_id = ?`, s.TrackID,\n\t); err != nil {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"delete old summary: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\n\tif _, err := duckDB.Exec(\n\t\t`INSERT INTO track_anomaly_summaries\n\t\t (track_id, computed_at, reading_count,\n\t\t  mean_doserate, median_doserate, stddev_doserate,\n\t\t  threshold, anomaly_count, anomaly_ids,\n\t\t  anomaly_max, anomaly_min)\n\t\t VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)`,\n\t\ts.TrackID, s.ComputedAt, s.ReadingCount,\n\t\ts.MeanDoserate, s.MedianDoserate, s.StddevDoserate,\n\t\ts.Threshold, s.AnomalyCount, string(idsJSON),\n\t\ts.AnomalyMax, s.AnomalyMin,\n\t); err != nil {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"insert summary: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\n\treturn nil\n}\n\n// loadAnomalySummary fetches the stored anomaly summary for a track from DuckLake.\n// Returns nil, nil if no summary has been computed yet.\nfunc loadAnomalySummary(trackID string) (*AnomalySummary, error) {\n\tif !duckDBAvailable() {\n\t\treturn nil, nil\n\t}\n\n\trow := duckDB.QueryRow(\n\t\t`SELECT track_id, computed_at, reading_count,\n\t\t        mean_doserate, median_doserate, stddev_doserate,\n\t\t        threshold, anomaly_count, anomaly_ids,\n\t\t        anomaly_max, anomaly_min\n\t\t FROM track_anomaly_summaries\n\t\t WHERE track_id = ?\n\t\t LIMIT 1`,\n\t\ttrackID,\n\t)\n\n\tvar s AnomalySummary\n\tvar idsJSON string\n\tif err := row.Scan(\n\t\t&s.TrackID, &s.ComputedAt, &s.ReadingCount,\n\t\t&s.MeanDoserate, &s.MedianDoserate, &s.StddevDoserate,\n\t\t&s.Threshold, &s.AnomalyCount, &idsJSON,\n\t\t&s.AnomalyMax, &s.AnomalyMin,\n\t); err != nil {\n\t\t// No row — not yet computed.\n\t\treturn nil, nil\n\t}\n\n\tif idsJSON != \"\" && idsJSON != \"null\" {\n\t\t_ = json.Unmarshal([]byte(idsJSON), &s.AnomalyIDs)\n\t}\n\n\treturn &s, nil\n}\n"
}

Clean build. Here's how to test it:

**1. Start the server:**
```bash
bash local-server-config.sh
```

**2. Upload a track** (any `.log` or `.csv` file via the upload page at `http://localhost:8765`)

**3. Watch the server logs** for the anomaly line — should appear ~2 seconds after upload completes:
```
[anomaly] <trackID>: 1234 readings, 3 anomalies (threshold 0.4521 µSv/h)
```

**4. Check the insights endpoint:**
```bash
curl -s "http://localhost:8765/api/track/<trackID>/insights" | jq .anomaly_summary
```

You should see the full stats: reading count, mean, median, stddev, threshold, and any flagged anomaly IDs.

> DEVELOPER

What are the parameters for something to be abnormal and are  the parametres for an admin configurable in the UI?

> AGENT

### Current Parameters (hardcoded)

In [track_anomaly.go](cmd/unified-server/track_anomaly.go):

| Parameter | Value | Meaning |
|-----------|-------|---------|
| Min valid doserate | `> 0` | Filters out zero/negative readings (sensor errors) |
| Max valid doserate | `< 10000 µSv/h` | Filters out obviously bogus sensor spikes |
| Anomaly threshold | `mean + 3×stddev` | Standard statistical outlier rule — ~0.3% of readings in a normal distribution |
| Max stored anomaly IDs | `50` | Caps the JSON array size |

So a reading is flagged as anomalous if it's **more than 3 standard deviations above the track's own mean**. This is relative to each track — a track measured near Fukushima will have a higher baseline than one in Tokyo, and anomalies are flagged relative to *that* track's own data.

---

### Admin Configurable?

**No** — currently nothing is configurable. These are compile-time constants buried in the code.

---

### Should I make them configurable?

I can add a small config section to the existing `/admin/translations`-style admin UI, or more simply add them as **server startup flags** (like `-anomaly-sigma 3.0 -anomaly-max-doserate 10000`). 

Which would you prefer?
- **Startup flags** — simple, no DB needed, requires restart to change
- **Admin UI** — changeable at runtime, stored in PostgreSQL, no restart needed

14		"id", "language_code", "key", "value", "updated_at",
15	}

17	// adminTranslationsDataHandler returns JSON data for the admin translations page.
18	// GET /api/admin/translations?limit=50&offset=0&sort=key&order=asc&search=...&lang=...
19	//
20	// @Summary     Admin translations list
21	// @Description Returns paginated translation rows for admin editing.
22	// @Tags        admin
23	// @Produce     json
24	// @Param       limit query int false "Page size"
25	// @Param       offset query int false "Offset"
26	// @Param       sort query string false "Sort column"
27	// @Param       order query string false "Sort order"
28	// @Param       search query string false "Search term"
29	// @Param       lang query string false "Language code filter"
30	// @Success     200 {object} map[string]interface{} "Translation rows"
31	// @Failure     503 {string} string "Database unavailable"
32	// @Router      /api/admin/translations [get]
33	func adminTranslationsDataHandler(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
34		if db == nil {
35			http.Error(w, "Database not available", http.StatusServiceUnavailable)
36			return
37		}
38	
39		limit, _ := strconv.Atoi(r.URL.Query().Get("limit"))
40		if limit <= 0 || limit > 1000 {
41			limit = 50
42		}
43		offset, _ := strconv.Atoi(r.URL.Query().Get("offset"))
44		if offset < 0 {
45			offset = 0
46		}
47	
48		sortCol := r.URL.Query().Get("sort")
49		if !isValidColumn(sortCol, translationColumns) {
50			sortCol = "key"
51		}
52		order := strings.ToUpper(r.URL.Query().Get("order"))
53		if order != "DESC" {
54			order = "ASC"
55		}
56	
57		search := r.URL.Query().Get("search")
58		langFilter := r.URL.Query().Get("lang")
59	
60		// Build WHERE clause
61		var conditions []string
62		var args []interface{}
63		argIdx := 1
64	
65		if langFilter != "" {
66			conditions = append(conditions, fmt.Sprintf("language_code = $%d", argIdx))
67			args = append(args, langFilter)
68			argIdx++
69		}
70		if search != "" {
71			conditions = append(conditions, fmt.Sprintf("(key ILIKE $%d OR value ILIKE $%d)", argIdx, argIdx+1))
72			args = append(args, "%"+search+"%", "%"+search+"%")
73			argIdx += 2
74		}
75	
76		whereSQL := ""
77		if len(conditions) > 0 {
78			whereSQL = "WHERE " + strings.Join(conditions, " AND ")
79		}
80

83		var total int
84		if err := db.DB.QueryRow(countQuery, args...).Scan(&total); err != nil {
85			log.Printf("admin translations count error: %v", err)
86			w.Header().Set("Content-Type", "application/json")
87			json.NewEncoder(w).Encode(map[string]interface{}{
88				"error": fmt.Sprintf("Query failed: %v", err),
89				"data":  []interface{}{},
90				"total": 0,
91			})
92			return
93		}
94	
95		// Fetch data
96		dataQuery := fmt.Sprintf("SELECT id, language_code, key, value, updated_at FROM translations %s ORDER BY %s %s LIMIT %d OFFSET %d",
97			whereSQL, sortCol, order, limit, offset)
98	
99		rows, err := db.DB.Query(dataQuery, args...)
100		if err != nil {
101			log.Printf("admin translations query error: %v", err)
102			http.Error(w, "Query failed", http.StatusInternalServerError)
103			return
104		}
105		defer rows.Close()
106	
107		var results []map[string]interface{}
108		for rows.Next() {
109			var id int64
110			var langCode, key, value string
111			var updatedAt interface{}
112			if err := rows.Scan(&id, &langCode, &key, &value, &updatedAt); err != nil {
113				log.Printf("admin translations scan error: %v", err)
114				continue
115			}
116			row := map[string]interface{}{
117				"id":            id,
118				"language_code": langCode,
119				"key":           key,
120				"value":         value,
121				"updated_at":    updatedAt,
122			}
123			results = append(results, row)
124		}
125	
126		if results == nil {
127			results = []map[string]interface{}{}
128		}
129	
130		// Get available languages for filter dropdown
131		langRows, err := db.DB.Query("SELECT DISTINCT language_code FROM translations ORDER BY language_code")
132		var languages []string
133		if err == nil {
134			defer langRows.Close()
135			for langRows.Next() {
136				var lang string
137				if langRows.Scan(&lang) == nil {
138					languages = append(languages, lang)
139				}
140			}
141		}
142	
143		w.Header().Set("Content-Type", "application/json")
144		json.NewEncoder(w).Encode(map[string]interface{}{
145			"data":      results,
146			"total":     total,
147			"languages": languages,
148		})
149	}

151	// adminTranslationUpdateHandler updates a single translation.
152	// PUT /api/admin/translations/{id}
153	//
154	// @Summary     Admin translation update
155	// @Description Updates translation value by translation row ID.
156	// @Tags        admin
157	// @Accept      json
158	// @Produce     json
159	// @Param       id path int true "Translation row ID"
160	// @Success     200 {object} map[string]string "Update result"
161	// @Failure     400 {string} string "Invalid request"
162	// @Failure     503 {string} string "Database unavailable"
163	// @Router      /api/admin/translations/{id} [put]
164	func adminTranslationUpdateHandler(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
165		if db == nil {
166			http.Error(w, "Database not available", http.StatusServiceUnavailable)
167			return
168		}
169	
170		// Extract ID from path: /api/admin/translations/123
171		parts := strings.Split(strings.TrimSuffix(r.URL.Path, "/"), "/")
172		if len(parts) < 4 {
173			http.Error(w, "Missing translation ID", http.StatusBadRequest)
174			return
175		}
176		idStr := parts[len(parts)-1]
177		id, err := strconv.ParseInt(idStr, 10, 64)
178		if err != nil {
179			http.Error(w, "Invalid translation ID", http.StatusBadRequest)
180			return
181		}
182	
183		var payload struct {
184			Value string `json:"value"`
185		}
186		if err := json.NewDecoder(r.Body).Decode(&payload); err != nil {
187			http.Error(w, "Invalid JSON", http.StatusBadRequest)
188			return
189		}
190	
191		_, err = db.DB.Exec("UPDATE translations SET value = $1, updated_at = NOW() WHERE id = $2", payload.Value, id)
192		if err != nil {
193			log.Printf("admin translation update error: %v", err)
194			http.Error(w, "Update failed", http.StatusInternalServerError)
195			return
196		}
197	
198		w.Header().Set("Content-Type", "application/json")
199		json.NewEncoder(w).Encode(map[string]string{"status": "ok"})

202	// adminTranslationCreateHandler creates a new translation.
203	// POST /api/admin/translations
204	//
205	// @Summary     Admin translation create
206	// @Description Creates a translation row or updates existing key/language pair.
207	// @Tags        admin
208	// @Accept      json
209	// @Produce     json
210	// @Success     201 {object} map[string]string "Create result"
211	// @Failure     400 {string} string "Invalid request"
212	// @Failure     503 {string} string "Database unavailable"
213	// @Router      /api/admin/translations [post]
214	func adminTranslationCreateHandler(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
215		if db == nil {
216			http.Error(w, "Database not available", http.StatusServiceUnavailable)
217			return
218		}
219	
220		var payload struct {
221			LanguageCode string `json:"language_code"`
222			Key          string `json:"key"`
223			Value        string `json:"value"`
224		}
225		if err := json.NewDecoder(r.Body).Decode(&payload); err != nil {
226			http.Error(w, "Invalid JSON", http.StatusBadRequest)
227			return
228		}
229	
230		if payload.LanguageCode == "" || payload.Key == "" {
231			http.Error(w, "language_code and key are required", http.StatusBadRequest)
232			return
233		}
234	
235		_, err := db.DB.Exec(
236			"INSERT INTO translations (language_code, key, value) VALUES ($1, $2, $3) ON CONFLICT (language_code, key) DO UPDATE SET value = EXCLUDED.value, updated_at = NOW()",
237			payload.LanguageCode, payload.Key, payload.Value,
238		)
239		if err != nil {
240			log.Printf("admin translation create error: %v", err)
241			http.Error(w, "Create failed", http.StatusInternalServerError)
242			return
243		}
244	
245		w.Header().Set("Content-Type", "application/json")
246		w.WriteHeader(http.StatusCreated)
247		json.NewEncoder(w).Encode(map[string]string{"status": "ok"})
248	}

252	//
253	// @Summary     Admin translation delete

255	// @Tags        admin

259	// @Failure     400 {string} string "Invalid request"
260	// @Failure     503 {string} string "Database unavailable"
261	// @Router      /api/admin/translations/{id} [delete]
262	func adminTranslationDeleteHandler(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
263		if db == nil {
264			http.Error(w, "Database not available", http.StatusServiceUnavailable)
265			return
266		}
267	
268		parts := strings.Split(strings.TrimSuffix(r.URL.Path, "/"), "/")
269		if len(parts) < 4 {
270			http.Error(w, "Missing translation ID", http.StatusBadRequest)
271			return
272		}
273		idStr := parts[len(parts)-1]
274		id, err := strconv.ParseInt(idStr, 10, 64)
275		if err != nil {
276			http.Error(w, "Invalid translation ID", http.StatusBadRequest)
277			return
278		}
279	
280		_, err = db.DB.Exec("DELETE FROM translations WHERE id = $1", id)

3	import (
4		"encoding/csv"

8		"net/http"
9		"strconv"
10		"strings"
11	)
12	
13	// adminMCPDataHandler returns JSON data for MCP analytics tables.
14	// GET /api/admin/mcp/data?table=chat_questions&limit=50&offset=0&sort=timestamp&order=desc&search=...
15	//
16	// @Summary     Admin MCP analytics data
17	// @Description Returns paginated MCP analytics rows for a selected table. For table=mcp_query_log, the columns are tool_name, created_at, duration_ms, result_count, client_info, and params.
18	// @Tags        admin
19	// @Produce     json
20	// @Param       table query string true "Analytics table name" Enums(chat_questions,mcp_query_log,mcp_ai_query_log)
21	// @Param       limit query int false "Page size"
22	// @Param       offset query int false "Offset"
23	// @Param       sort query string false "Sort column (for mcp_query_log, default is created_at)"
24	// @Param       order query string false "Sort order: asc or desc"
25	// @Param       search query string false "Search term"
26	// @Success     200 {object} map[string]interface{} "Analytics rows"
27	// @Failure     400 {string} string "Invalid table"
28	// @Failure     503 {string} string "Analytics unavailable"
29	// @Router      /api/admin/mcp/data [get]
30	func adminMCPDataHandler(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
31		if !duckDBAvailable() {
32			http.Error(w, "Analytics not available", http.StatusServiceUnavailable)
33			return
34		}
35	
36		tableName := r.URL.Query().Get("table")
37		columns, ok := mcpTableColumns[tableName]
38		if !ok {
39			http.Error(w, "Invalid table name", http.StatusBadRequest)
40			return
41		}
42	
43		limit, _ := strconv.Atoi(r.URL.Query().Get("limit"))
44		if limit <= 0 || limit > 1000 {
45			limit = 50
46		}
47		offset, _ := strconv.Atoi(r.URL.Query().Get("offset"))
48		if offset < 0 {
49			offset = 0
50		}
51	
52		sortCol := r.URL.Query().Get("sort")
53		if !isValidColumn(sortCol, columns) {
54			switch tableName {
55			case "mcp_query_log":
56				sortCol = "created_at"
57			case "qa_embeddings":
58				sortCol = "feedback_score"
59			case "location_knowledge":
60				sortCol = "created_at"
```

156	</nav>
157	<div class="page-content">
158	
159	<div class="admin-tabs" id="adminTabs"></div>
160	
161	<div class="controls">
162	  <select id="langFilter" onchange="filterByLang()">
163	    <option value="">All languages</option>
164	  </select>
165	  <input type="text" id="searchInput" placeholder="Search key or value..." onkeydown="if(event.key==='Enter')doSearch()">
166	  <button onclick="doSearch()">Search</button>
167	  <button onclick="clearSearch()">Clear</button>
168	  <button class="add-btn" onclick="showAddModal()">+ Add Translation</button>
169	  <button class="reload-btn" onclick="reloadTranslations()">Reload into Memory</button>
170	  <span class="summary" id="summary"></span>
171	</div>

173	<div class="table-wrap">

175	  <table id="dataTable" style="display:none">
176	    <thead>
177	      <tr>
178	        <th class="col-id sortable" onclick="sortBy('id')">ID</th>
179	        <th class="col-lang sortable" onclick="sortBy('language_code')">Lang</th>
180	        <th class="col-key sortable" onclick="sortBy('key')">Key</th>
181	        <th class="col-value">Value</th>
182	        <th class="col-date sortable" onclick="sortBy('updated_at')">Updated</th>
183	        <th class="col-actions">Actions</th>
184	      </tr>
185	    </thead>

187	  </table>

189	</div>
