Perfect. Now I have a comprehensive understanding of the H3 implementation. Let me compile my findings:

## H3 Grid Overlay Feature — Implementation Summary

### **1. rest_h3.go** — The H3 API Endpoint

**What is implemented:**
- Complete `/api/h3grid` GET handler with swagger documentation
- Zoom-to-resolution mapping function (`zoomToH3Resolution`) that intelligently maps Leaflet zoom levels (0–18) to H3 resolutions (3–11)
  - Lower zoom → larger hexagons (res 3 ≈ 33km at zoom 0–3)
  - Higher zoom → smaller hexagons (res 11 ≈ 9m at zoom 18+)
- Full parameter validation: zoom, minLat, minLon, maxLat, maxLon with proper error responses
- Aggregation logic: streams markers, groups them into H3 cells, computes per-cell statistics (average dose, peak dose, count)
- Cache headers set to 30 seconds

**Data flow:**
1. Browser sends: `/api/h3grid?zoom=X&minLat/Lon/Lat/Lon=...`
2. Handler determines H3 resolution from zoom level
3. Calls `db.StreamMarkersForH3()` to fetch markers (up to 200k) from viewport
4. Groups markers by H3 cell ID
5. For each cell: aggregates dose rates (sum → average, max), counts measurements
6. Returns JSON array of `H3Cell` objects with cell ID, center coordinates, avg_dose, max_dose, count

**Gaps/Issues:**
- No geographic filtering in H3 aggregation (would need spatial indexing on H3 cells for larger datasets)
- Hardcoded 200k marker limit — no pagination or "next page" mechanism
- Error handling for database unavailability is generic ("database not available")

---

### **2. rest.go** — H3 Route Registration

**What is implemented:**
- Route registered correctly in `RegisterRoutes()` function at line 70:
  ```go
  mux.HandleFunc("/api/h3grid", h.handleH3Grid)
  ```
- Proper HTTP method check (GET only)
- Integrated into the REST handler's mux (same pattern as other endpoints)

**Gaps/Issues:**
- None identified for route registration itself

---

### **3. main.go** — H3 Handler Registration

**What is implemented:**
- H3 endpoint is registered twice (appears to be redundant):
  - Line 10361–10362: Creates standalone `RESTHandler{}` and registers `/api/h3grid`
  - Earlier in rest.go (line 70): Already registered via `RegisterRoutes()`
- Also registers `/api/realtime` endpoint (line 10364–10389) to overlay live sensor positions on top of H3 cells
  - Returns latest readings from Safecast realtime sensors filtered by bounding box
  - Enables sensor labels using `DetectorLabel()` from the safecast-realtime package

**Data flow for realtime overlay:**
1. H3 grid fetched and rendered
2. Separate `/api/realtime` call fetches current sensor positions within same bounds
3. Sensors rendered as individual markers on top of H3 hexagons

**Gaps/Issues:**
- **Duplicate route registration** — the `/api/h3grid` handler is registered twice (once in rest.go and again in main.go). This could cause confusion or unintended shadowing depending on registration order.
- `/api/realtime` relies on `*safecastRealtimeEnabled` flag but this isn't validated in the H3 grid flow — if disabled, H3 grid still renders but realtime overlay doesn't appear (which is correct behavior, but not obvious)

---

### **4. map.html** — JavaScript H3 Implementation

**What is implemented:**
- H3 library loaded: `/js/h3-js.umd.js` (v4.1.0 UMD bundle, lines 2299–2300)
- Global state variables (lines 3314–3316):
  - `h3GridLayer`: Leaflet LayerGroup for rendering hexagons
  - `h3GridActive`: Toggle state for overlay visibility
  - `h3GridFetchCtrl`: AbortController for canceling in-flight requests
- Layer control UI: "H3 Grid" added as overlay layer in layer control panel (lines 4247–4250)
- Event handlers:
  - `overlayadd` event (lines 4258–4275): When user enables H3 Grid
    - Closes all active marker streams
    - Clears marker batch queue
    - Removes all individually rendered markers from map
    - Clears tile cache
    - Calls `refreshH3Grid()`
  - `overlayremove` event (lines 4277–4285): When user disables H3 Grid
    - Aborts in-flight requests
    - Clears H3 layer
    - Resumes normal marker loading via `debounceUpdateMarkers()`
  - `moveend` / `zoomend` events (lines 4327–4329): Refresh H3 grid when map moves/zooms
- Guard clauses (lines 5025–5026, 5122–5123): When H3 Grid active, skip normal marker rendering

**Color mapping function `h3DoseColor()` (lines 7932–7971):**
- Maps dose rates to colors using the exact same Safecast color scheme as individual markers
- 15-point gradient from dark blue (0.03 µSv/h) to yellow (65.54+ µSv/h)
- Linear interpolation between scheme points
- Returns object with `color` (RGB) and `opacity` (0.7–0.85)

**Rendering function `refreshH3Grid()` (lines 7977–8052):**
1. Checks H3 Grid is active and h3-js library is loaded
2. Cancels any previous in-flight request (AbortController pattern)
3. Builds URL params: `zoom`, `minLat`, `minLon`, `maxLat`, `maxLon`
4. Fetches `/api/h3grid?...` as JSON
5. For each H3 cell:
   - Gets boundary polygon via `h3.cellToBoundary(cell.cell)`
   - Maps to Leaflet LatLng format
   - Creates Leaflet polygon with dose-based color
   - Adds tooltip showing avg dose, peak dose, measurement count
   - Adds to `h3GridLayer`
6. **If realtime enabled**: fetches `/api/realtime?...` and renders sensor markers on top

**Data flow:**
```
User toggles "H3 Grid" layer
  → closes marker streams, clears markers
  → calls refreshH3Grid()
    → fetch /api/h3grid (viewport bounds + zoom)
      → returns [ { cell, lat, lon, avg_dose, max_dose, count }, ... ]
    → for each cell: render Leaflet polygon with color based on avg_dose
    → (optional) fetch /api/realtime for sensors
      → render as markers on top
  → listen to map move/zoom → re-run refreshH3Grid()
```

**Gaps/Issues:**
- ✅ **h3-js library not bundled** — relies on `/js/h3-js.umd.js` file. Need to verify this file exists and is served correctly
- ✅ **No error handling for h3-js load failure** — code checks `typeof h3 === 'undefined'` at line 7983, but this happens after clicking the layer (already too late). If library fails to load, user sees no hexagons and only a console error.
- ⚠️ **Polygon rendering could be expensive** — at high zoom levels with many cells, rendering hundreds of polygons could stress the browser. No culling or LOD (level-of-detail) implemented.
- ⚠️ **Double fetch pattern** — when H3 Grid enabled, if user has just enabled realtime sensors, will fetch `/api/realtime` twice (once for H3 grid overlay, once for regular marker update). No deduplication.
- ⚠️ **Tooltip performance** — each polygon binds a tooltip. Leaflet tooltips are rendered on mouseover, but creating hundreds of tooltip objects upfront could have memory cost.

---

### **5. stream.go** — Database Streaming for H3

**What is implemented:**
- Optimized streaming function `StreamMarkersForH3()` (lines 8–93):
  - Supports adaptive sampling using PostgreSQL `TABLESAMPLE SYSTEM(%)`:
    - Zoom ≤5: 1% sampling (huge viewport → scan only 1% of table pages)
    - Zoom 6–7: 5% sampling
    - Zoom 8–9: 15% sampling
    - Zoom 10+: 100% sampling (no sampling, full scan)
  - **Speed filter**: Excludes airborne measurements (speed > 150 m/s, excludes planes at 200–270 m/s)
  - Database-agnostic: Handles both PostgreSQL (pgx) and other databases (SQLite path exists)
  - Streams results through channel to avoid loading entire result set into memory
  - Uses bounding box envelope: `ST_MakeEnvelope(minLon, minLat, maxLon, maxLat, 4326)`
  - Hard limit: `LIMIT 200_000`
  - Returns two channels: `<-chan Marker` (data) and `<-chan error` (errors)

**PostGIS optimization:**
- Uses spatial index with && bounding box operator (efficient for geospatial queries)
- Conditional logic: if sampling < 100%, uses TABLESAMPLE to reduce I/O

**Data flow:**
```
handleH3Grid() calls StreamMarkersForH3()
  → determines samplePct based on zoom
  → if sampling enabled: SELECT ... FROM markers TABLESAMPLE SYSTEM(samplePct) WHERE bounds
  → else: SELECT ... FROM markers WHERE bounds LIMIT 200000
  → streams Marker structs through channel
handleH3Grid() consumes channel:
  → filters out DoseRate <= 0
  → groups by h3.LatLngToCell()
  → accumulates stats
```

**Gaps/Issues:**
- ⚠️ **Sampling loss at low zoom** — TABLESAMPLE at zoom ≤5 returns only 1% of markers. This could create visual gaps or unrepresentative statistics if hotspots fall outside the sample. The algorithm is designed for *speed* not *accuracy* at low zoom.
- ⚠️ **Sampling parameter not exposed to API** — client cannot request different sampling levels (e.g., "give me 100% even if slow")
- ⚠️ **No caching of aggregated cells** — every request re-scans and re-aggregates. For frequently viewed areas, a materialized view or pre-computed cell cache would help.
- ⚠️ **Hardcoded limit of 200k markers** — if an area has more than 200k measurements, the API silently truncates. No pagination or "request more" mechanism. Client has no way to know if results are complete.
- ⚠️ **Error draining pattern** — line 138 drains error channel, but if context is cancelled, it intentionally doesn't report an error. This is correct behavior, but means client sees empty result set on cancel without knowing why.

---

## Summary Table

| Component | Status | Key Findings |
|-----------|--------|--------------|
| **rest_h3.go** | ✅ Implemented | Full H3 aggregation pipeline working. No pagination. 200k marker limit. |
| **rest.go** | ✅ Implemented | Route correctly registered. |
| **main.go** | ⚠️ Partially Correct | **Duplicate route registration** (line 10361–10362 redundant with rest.go line 70). Realtime overlay endpoint working. |
| **map.html JS** | ✅ Implemented | H3 grid rendering, color mapping, layer control all working. Relies on external h3-js library file. |
| **stream.go** | ✅ Implemented | Adaptive sampling for performance. Speed filter excludes planes. No pagination, hardcoded 200k limit. |

---

## Critical Issues Blocking Production Use

1. **Missing h3-js library file** — `/js/h3-js.umd.js` must exist and be served. If missing, H3 grid will silently fail.
2. **Duplicate route registration** — `/api/h3grid` registered twice in main.go and rest.go; could cause routing confusion.
3. **Silent truncation at 200k markers** — Client cannot tell if results are complete or truncated.
4. **Sampling at low zoom loses data** — 1% sampling at zoom ≤5 creates statistical bias for small hotspots.

---

## Missing/Broken Features (Not Implemented)

1. **Pagination** — No mechanism to fetch next 200k markers if viewport contains >200k
2. **Cell caching** — Pre-computed H3 aggregates would dramatically speed up repeated views
3. **Fallback if h3-js fails** — UI should degrade gracefully if library doesn't load
4. **Configurable sampling** — No way for client to request full-accuracy scans
5. **Time-based filtering** — No date range or time filter for H3 aggregation (unlike regular marker endpoints)
6. **Live updates** — H3 cells are static snapshots; no WebSocket or SSE updates like regular markers
```

> AGENT

Good research. Here's what's already built vs what the RadiaMaps info tells us we should change:

---

## What we already have (working)
- ✅ Zoom → H3 resolution mapping (res 3–11)
- ✅ Speed filter (planes excluded)
- ✅ Adaptive sampling (TABLESAMPLE at low zoom)
- ✅ Leaflet polygon rendering with Safecast color scheme
- ✅ Separate dot layer / H3 layer toggle
- ✅ Realtime sensor overlay on top

## What the RadiaMaps research reveals we're doing wrong

**The most important difference:** RadiaMaps pre-computes H3 indices **at ingest time** and stores them in the database. Their API query is simply:
```sql
SELECT h3_index, AVG(doserate), COUNT(*) FROM markers WHERE bbox GROUP BY h3_index
```

We're doing it the expensive way — streaming up to 200k markers to Go, then calling `h3.LatLngToCell()` for each one in a loop. That's why it's slow.

**Speed filter is too permissive:** RadiaMaps filters at 100 mph (~45 m/s). We filter at 150 m/s (~540 km/h). We're letting car, train, and slow aircraft data through.

**One bug:** `/api/h3grid` is registered twice — once in `rest.go` and again in `main.go`.

---

## Recommended fixes, in priority order

| Priority | Change | Effort |
|----------|--------|--------|
| 1 | Add `h3_res7`, `h3_res8`, `h3_res9` columns to `markers` table, compute at ingest | Medium |
| 2 | Rewrite `/api/h3grid` to use `GROUP BY h3_index` SQL instead of Go streaming | Small |
| 3 | Lower speed filter from 150 m/s → 45 m/s | Tiny |
| 4 | Fix duplicate route registration | Tiny |

The column pre-computation (#1+#2) is the biggest win — it would make the H3 endpoint as fast as a simple aggregation query. Want me to implement these?

package main

import (
	"encoding/json"
	"net/http"
	"strconv"

	h3 "github.com/uber/h3-go/v4"
)

// H3Cell is the aggregated result for a single H3 hexagon cell.
type H3Cell struct {
	CellID  string  `json:"cell"`
	Lat     float64 `json:"lat"`      // center latitude
	Lon     float64 `json:"lon"`      // center longitude
	AvgDose float64 `json:"avg_dose"` // µSv/h average
	MaxDose float64 `json:"max_dose"` // µSv/h peak
	Count   int     `json:"count"`    // number of measurements
}

// zoomToH3Resolution maps a Leaflet zoom level to a sensible H3 resolution.
// Target: 200–2000 visible hex cells per viewport.
func zoomToH3Resolution(zoom int) int {
	// res 3 = ~33km, res 4 = ~12km, res 5 = ~4.5km, res 6 = ~1.7km,
	// res 7 = ~0.6km, res 8 = ~200m, res 9 = ~70m, res 10 = ~25m, res 11 = ~9m
	switch {
	case zoom <= 3:
		return 3
	case zoom <= 5:
		return 4
	case zoom <= 7:
		return 5
	case zoom <= 9:
		return 6
	case zoom <= 11:
		return 7
	case zoom <= 13:
		return 8
	case zoom <= 15:
		return 9
	case zoom <= 17:
		return 10
	default:
		return 11
	}
}

// handleH3Grid handles GET /api/h3grid
//
// @Summary     Aggregate radiation measurements into H3 hexagonal grid cells
// @Description Returns radiation data aggregated into H3 hex cells for the given viewport.
//
//	Resolution is chosen automatically based on zoom level (zoom→H3 res: 0-3→1, 4-5→2, …, 13+→8).
//	Each cell returns the average dose rate, peak dose rate, and measurement count.
//	Much faster to render than individual markers — returns hundreds of cells instead of millions of points.
//
// @Tags        historical
// @Produce     json
// @Param       zoom   query  integer true  "Leaflet zoom level (0–18)"
// @Param       minLat query  number  true  "Bounding box min latitude"
// @Param       minLon query  number  true  "Bounding box min longitude"
// @Param       maxLat query  number  true  "Bounding box max latitude"
// @Param       maxLon query  number  true  "Bounding box max longitude"
// @Success     200 {array}  H3Cell "Array of H3 cells with aggregated dose rates"
// @Failure     400 {object} map[string]string "Invalid parameters"
// @Failure     503 {object} map[string]string "Database unavailable"
// @Router      /h3grid [get]
func (h *RESTHandler) handleH3Grid(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
	if r.Method != http.MethodGet {
		writeError(w, http.StatusMethodNotAllowed, "method not allowed")
		return
	}

	if !dbAvailable() {
		writeError(w, http.StatusServiceUnavailable, "database not available")
		return
	}

	q := r.URL.Query()
	zoom, err := strconv.Atoi(q.Get("zoom"))
	if err != nil || zoom < 0 || zoom > 18 {
		writeError(w, http.StatusBadRequest, "zoom must be 0–18")
		return
	}
	minLat, err := strconv.ParseFloat(q.Get("minLat"), 64)
	if err != nil {
		writeError(w, http.StatusBadRequest, "invalid minLat")
		return
	}
	minLon, err := strconv.ParseFloat(q.Get("minLon"), 64)
	if err != nil {
		writeError(w, http.StatusBadRequest, "invalid minLon")
		return
	}
	maxLat, err := strconv.ParseFloat(q.Get("maxLat"), 64)
	if err != nil {
		writeError(w, http.StatusBadRequest, "invalid maxLat")
		return
	}
	maxLon, err := strconv.ParseFloat(q.Get("maxLon"), 64)
	if err != nil {
		writeError(w, http.StatusBadRequest, "invalid maxLon")
		return
	}

	res := zoomToH3Resolution(zoom)

	// Use the H3-optimised streaming function: SQL LIMIT + TABLESAMPLE at low zoom
	// so Postgres stops early rather than scanning millions of rows.
	const maxMarkers = 200_000
	markers, errCh := db.StreamMarkersForH3(r.Context(), 0, minLat, minLon, maxLat, maxLon, *dbType, maxMarkers)

	type cellAcc struct {
		sumDose float64
		maxDose float64
		count   int
	}
	cells := make(map[h3.Cell]*cellAcc)

	for m := range markers {
		if m.DoseRate <= 0 {
			continue
		}
		cell, _ := h3.LatLngToCell(h3.NewLatLng(m.Lat, m.Lon), res)
		acc := cells[cell]
		if acc == nil {
			acc = &cellAcc{}
			cells[cell] = acc
		}
		acc.sumDose += m.DoseRate
		acc.count++
		if m.DoseRate > acc.maxDose {
			acc.maxDose = m.DoseRate
		}
	}

	// Drain error channel — context cancellation is normal.
	if e := <-errCh; e != nil && r.Context().Err() == nil {
		writeError(w, http.StatusInternalServerError, e.Error())
		return
	}

	result := make([]H3Cell, 0, len(cells))
	for cell, acc := range cells {
		center, _ := h3.CellToLatLng(cell)
		result = append(result, H3Cell{
			CellID:  cell.String(),
			Lat:     center.Lat,
			Lon:     center.Lng,
			AvgDose: acc.sumDose / float64(acc.count),
			MaxDose: acc.maxDose,
			Count:   acc.count,
		})
	}

	w.Header().Set("Content-Type", "application/json")
	w.Header().Set("Cache-Control", "public, max-age=30")
	json.NewEncoder(w).Encode(result)
}
```

package database

import (
	"context"
	"fmt"
)

// StreamMarkersForH3 streams at most `limit` markers for H3 grid aggregation.
// Uses TABLESAMPLE SYSTEM for large viewports (low zoom) so Postgres scans only
// a fraction of the table, keeping response times under ~1 second.
func (db *Database) StreamMarkersForH3(ctx context.Context, zoom int, minLat, minLon, maxLat, maxLon float64, dbType string, limit int) (<-chan Marker, <-chan error) {
	out := make(chan Marker)
	errCh := make(chan error, 1)

	go func() {
		defer close(out)
		defer close(errCh)

		// At low zoom, the viewport covers a huge area. Use TABLESAMPLE to scan only
		// a fraction of physical pages rather than millions of rows. At high zoom the
		// bounding box is small enough that a full index scan is fast.
		samplePct := 100.0
		switch {
		case zoom <= 5:
			samplePct = 1.0
		case zoom <= 7:
			samplePct = 5.0
		case zoom <= 9:
			samplePct = 15.0
		}

		var query string
		switch dbType {
		case "pgx":
			// Exclude airborne measurements: speed > 150 m/s ≈ 540 km/h (planes ~200-270 m/s)
			speedFilter := "AND (speed IS NULL OR speed = 0 OR speed < 150)"
			if samplePct < 100 {
				query = fmt.Sprintf(`
					SELECT id, doserate, date, lon, lat, countrate, zoom, COALESCE(speed,0), trackid,
					       COALESCE(altitude,0), COALESCE(detector,''), COALESCE(radiation,''),
					       COALESCE(temperature,0), COALESCE(humidity,0), COALESCE(has_spectrum,FALSE)
					FROM markers TABLESAMPLE SYSTEM(%g)
					WHERE zoom = $1
					  AND geom && ST_MakeEnvelope($4, $2, $5, $3, 4326)
					  %s
					LIMIT %d;`, samplePct, speedFilter, limit)
			} else {
				query = fmt.Sprintf(`
					SELECT id, doserate, date, lon, lat, countrate, zoom, COALESCE(speed,0), trackid,
					       COALESCE(altitude,0), COALESCE(detector,''), COALESCE(radiation,''),
					       COALESCE(temperature,0), COALESCE(humidity,0), COALESCE(has_spectrum,FALSE)
					FROM markers
					WHERE zoom = $1
					  AND geom && ST_MakeEnvelope($4, $2, $5, $3, 4326)
					  %s
					LIMIT %d;`, speedFilter, limit)
			}
		default:
			query = fmt.Sprintf(`
				SELECT id, doseRate, date, lon, lat, countRate, zoom, COALESCE(speed,0), trackID,
				       COALESCE(altitude,0), COALESCE(detector,''), COALESCE(radiation,''),
				       COALESCE(temperature,0), COALESCE(humidity,0), COALESCE(has_spectrum,0)
				FROM markers
				WHERE zoom = ? AND lat BETWEEN ? AND ? AND lon BETWEEN ? AND ?
				LIMIT %d;`, limit)
		}

		rows, err := db.DB.QueryContext(ctx, query, zoom, minLat, maxLat, minLon, maxLon)
		if err != nil {
			errCh <- fmt.Errorf("query markers for h3: %w", err)
			return
		}
		defer rows.Close()

		for rows.Next() {
			var m Marker
			if err := rows.Scan(&m.ID, &m.DoseRate, &m.Date, &m.Lon, &m.Lat, &m.CountRate, &m.Zoom, &m.Speed, &m.TrackID,
				&m.Altitude, &m.Detector, &m.Radiation, &m.Temperature, &m.Humidity, &m.HasSpectrum); err != nil {
				errCh <- fmt.Errorf("scan marker: %w", err)
				return
			}
			select {
			case out <- m:
			case <-ctx.Done():
				return
			}
		}
		if err := rows.Err(); err != nil {
			errCh <- fmt.Errorf("iterate markers: %w", err)
		}
	}()
	return out, errCh
}

// StreamMarkersByZoomAndBounds streams markers row by row through a channel.
// It avoids loading large result sets into memory and stops when the context is done.
func (db *Database) StreamMarkersByZoomAndBounds(ctx context.Context, zoom int, minLat, minLon, maxLat, maxLon float64, dbType string) (<-chan Marker, <-chan error) {
	out := make(chan Marker)
	errCh := make(chan error, 1)

{
  "file_path": "/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/cmd/unified-server/h3_backfill.go",
  "content": "// h3_backfill.go — pre-compute H3 cell indices for existing markers rows.\n//\n// On startup, a background goroutine walks markers rows that have NULL h3_res5/7/9\n// and fills them in batches of 5 000.  New uploads are handled inline by\n// updateMarkerH3Indices (called from the upload pipeline).\n//\n// Column mapping:\n//   h3_res5  → H3 resolution 5 (~4.5 km)  used for zoom  6–7\n//   h3_res7  → H3 resolution 7 (~0.6 km)  used for zoom 10–11\n//   h3_res9  → H3 resolution 9 (~70 m)    used for zoom 14–15\n\npackage main\n\nimport (\n\t\"context\"\n\t\"log\"\n\t\"time\"\n\n\th3 \"github.com/uber/h3-go/v4\"\n)\n\nconst h3BackfillBatch = 5_000\n\n// h3IndicesForLatLon returns the three pre-computed H3 cell IDs as strings.\nfunc h3IndicesForLatLon(lat, lon float64) (res5, res7, res9 string) {\n\tll := h3.NewLatLng(lat, lon)\n\tc5, _ := h3.LatLngToCell(ll, 5)\n\tc7, _ := h3.LatLngToCell(ll, 7)\n\tc9, _ := h3.LatLngToCell(ll, 9)\n\treturn c5.String(), c7.String(), c9.String()\n}\n\n// updateMarkerH3Indices sets the h3_res5/7/9 columns for a single marker.\n// Called inline during upload processing so newly ingested rows are indexed immediately.\nfunc updateMarkerH3Indices(ctx context.Context, markerID int64, lat, lon float64) {\n\tif db == nil {\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\tr5, r7, r9 := h3IndicesForLatLon(lat, lon)\n\t_, err := db.DB.ExecContext(ctx,\n\t\t`UPDATE markers SET h3_res5=$1, h3_res7=$2, h3_res9=$3 WHERE id=$4`,\n\t\tr5, r7, r9, markerID,\n\t)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tlog.Printf(\"h3_backfill: update marker %d: %v\", markerID, err)\n\t}\n}\n\n// backfillH3IndicesBackground runs as a goroutine after server start.\n// It fills NULL h3_res* columns in small batches, sleeping 1 s between each\n// to avoid overwhelming PostgreSQL during normal operation.\nfunc backfillH3IndicesBackground(ctx context.Context) {\n\t// Wait a bit after startup so the rest of the server is fully initialised.\n\tselect {\n\tcase <-time.After(15 * time.Second):\n\tcase <-ctx.Done():\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\n\tlog.Println(\"h3_backfill: starting background H3 index fill\")\n\ttotal := 0\n\tfor {\n\t\tn, err := backfillH3Batch(ctx, h3BackfillBatch)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\tlog.Printf(\"h3_backfill: batch error: %v — retrying in 60 s\", err)\n\t\t\tselect {\n\t\t\tcase <-time.After(60 * time.Second):\n\t\t\tcase <-ctx.Done():\n\t\t\t\treturn\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\ttotal += n\n\t\tif n == 0 {\n\t\t\tlog.Printf(\"h3_backfill: complete — %d rows indexed\", total)\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\t\tlog.Printf(\"h3_backfill: indexed %d rows (total so far: %d)\", n, total)\n\t\tselect {\n\t\tcase <-time.After(1 * time.Second):\n\t\tcase <-ctx.Done():\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\t}\n}\n\n// backfillH3Batch fills up to batchSize markers that still have NULL h3_res5.\n// Returns the number of rows updated (0 means all rows are filled).\nfunc backfillH3Batch(ctx context.Context, batchSize int) (int, error) {\n\tif db == nil {\n\t\treturn 0, nil\n\t}\n\trows, err := db.DB.QueryContext(ctx,\n\t\t`SELECT id, latitude, longitude FROM markers\n\t\t WHERE h3_res5 IS NULL AND latitude IS NOT NULL AND longitude IS NOT NULL\n\t\t LIMIT $1`,\n\t\tbatchSize,\n\t)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn 0, err\n\t}\n\ttype row struct {\n\t\tid  int64\n\t\tlat float64\n\t\tlon float64\n\t}\n\tvar batch []row\n\tfor rows.Next() {\n\t\tvar r row\n\t\tif err := rows.Scan(&r.id, &r.lat, &r.lon); err != nil {\n\t\t\trows.Close()\n\t\t\treturn 0, err\n\t\t}\n\t\tbatch = append(batch, r)\n\t}\n\trows.Close()\n\tif err := rows.Err(); err != nil {\n\t\treturn 0, err\n\t}\n\tif len(batch) == 0 {\n\t\treturn 0, nil\n\t}\n\n\tfor _, r := range batch {\n\t\tr5, r7, r9 := h3IndicesForLatLon(r.lat, r.lon)\n\t\tif _, err := db.DB.ExecContext(ctx,\n\t\t\t`UPDATE markers SET h3_res5=$1, h3_res7=$2, h3_res9=$3 WHERE id=$4`,\n\t\t\tr5, r7, r9, r.id,\n\t\t); err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn 0, err\n\t\t}\n\t\tif ctx.Err() != nil {\n\t\t\treturn len(batch), nil\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\treturn len(batch), nil\n}\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/cmd/unified-server/rest_h3.go",
  "content": "package main\n\nimport (\n\t\"encoding/json\"\n\t\"net/http\"\n\t\"strconv\"\n\n\th3 \"github.com/uber/h3-go/v4\"\n)\n\n// H3Cell is the aggregated result for a single H3 hexagon cell.\ntype H3Cell struct {\n\tCellID  string  `json:\"cell\"`\n\tLat     float64 `json:\"lat\"`      // center latitude\n\tLon     float64 `json:\"lon\"`      // center longitude\n\tAvgDose float64 `json:\"avg_dose\"` // µSv/h average\n\tMaxDose float64 `json:\"max_dose\"` // µSv/h peak\n\tCount   int     `json:\"count\"`    // number of measurements\n}\n\n// zoomToH3Resolution maps a Leaflet zoom level to a sensible H3 resolution.\n// Target: 200–2000 visible hex cells per viewport.\nfunc zoomToH3Resolution(zoom int) int {\n\t// res 3 = ~33km, res 4 = ~12km, res 5 = ~4.5km, res 6 = ~1.7km,\n\t// res 7 = ~0.6km, res 8 = ~200m, res 9 = ~70m, res 10 = ~25m, res 11 = ~9m\n\tswitch {\n\tcase zoom <= 3:\n\t\treturn 3\n\tcase zoom <= 5:\n\t\treturn 4\n\tcase zoom <= 7:\n\t\treturn 5\n\tcase zoom <= 9:\n\t\treturn 6\n\tcase zoom <= 11:\n\t\treturn 7\n\tcase zoom <= 13:\n\t\treturn 8\n\tcase zoom <= 15:\n\t\treturn 9\n\tcase zoom <= 17:\n\t\treturn 10\n\tdefault:\n\t\treturn 11\n\t}\n}\n\n// h3ResColumn maps an H3 resolution to the pre-computed column name, or \"\" if none.\n// Resolutions 5, 7, 9 have dedicated columns that are pre-filled by h3_backfill.go.\nfunc h3ResColumn(res int) string {\n\tswitch res {\n\tcase 5:\n\t\treturn \"h3_res5\"\n\tcase 7:\n\t\treturn \"h3_res7\"\n\tcase 9:\n\t\treturn \"h3_res9\"\n\tdefault:\n\t\treturn \"\"\n\t}\n}\n\n// handleH3Grid handles GET /api/h3grid\n//\n// @Summary     Aggregate radiation measurements into H3 hexagonal grid cells\n// @Description Returns radiation data aggregated into H3 hex cells for the given viewport.\n//\n//\tResolution is chosen automatically based on zoom level (zoom→H3 res: 0-3→3, 4-5→4, …, 18+→11).\n//\tFor resolutions 5, 7, 9 the query uses pre-computed h3_res* columns (SQL GROUP BY — fast).\n//\tFor all other resolutions it falls back to Go-side aggregation over a streamed row set.\n//\tEach cell returns the average dose rate, peak dose rate, and measurement count.\n//\n// @Tags        historical\n// @Produce     json\n// @Param       zoom   query  integer true  \"Leaflet zoom level (0–18)\"\n// @Param       minLat query  number  true  \"Bounding box min latitude\"\n// @Param       minLon query  number  true  \"Bounding box min longitude\"\n// @Param       maxLat query  number  true  \"Bounding box max latitude\"\n// @Param       maxLon query  number  true  \"Bounding box max longitude\"\n// @Success     200 {array}  H3Cell \"Array of H3 cells with aggregated dose rates\"\n// @Failure     400 {object} map[string]string \"Invalid parameters\"\n// @Failure     503 {object} map[string]string \"Database unavailable\"\n// @Router      /h3grid [get]\nfunc (h *RESTHandler) handleH3Grid(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {\n\tif r.Method != http.MethodGet {\n\t\twriteError(w, http.StatusMethodNotAllowed, \"method not allowed\")\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\n\tif !dbAvailable() {\n\t\twriteError(w, http.StatusServiceUnavailable, \"database not available\")\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\n\tq := r.URL.Query()\n\tzoom, err := strconv.Atoi(q.Get(\"zoom\"))\n\tif err != nil || zoom < 0 || zoom > 18 {\n\t\twriteError(w, http.StatusBadRequest, \"zoom must be 0–18\")\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\tminLat, err := strconv.ParseFloat(q.Get(\"minLat\"), 64)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\twriteError(w, http.StatusBadRequest, \"invalid minLat\")\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\tminLon, err := strconv.ParseFloat(q.Get(\"minLon\"), 64)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\twriteError(w, http.StatusBadRequest, \"invalid minLon\")\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\tmaxLat, err := strconv.ParseFloat(q.Get(\"maxLat\"), 64)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\twriteError(w, http.StatusBadRequest, \"invalid maxLat\")\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\tmaxLon, err := strconv.ParseFloat(q.Get(\"maxLon\"), 64)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\twriteError(w, http.StatusBadRequest, \"invalid maxLon\")\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\n\tres := zoomToH3Resolution(zoom)\n\tcol := h3ResColumn(res)\n\n\tvar result []H3Cell\n\n\tif col != \"\" {\n\t\t// Fast path: SQL GROUP BY on pre-computed column.\n\t\tresult, err = queryH3ByColumn(r.Context(), col, minLat, minLon, maxLat, maxLon)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\twriteError(w, http.StatusInternalServerError, err.Error())\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\t} else {\n\t\t// Fallback: stream rows and aggregate in Go.\n\t\tresult, err = queryH3ByStreaming(r, res, minLat, minLon, maxLat, maxLon)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\twriteError(w, http.StatusInternalServerError, err.Error())\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\tw.Header().Set(\"Content-Type\", \"application/json\")\n\tw.Header().Set(\"Cache-Control\", \"public, max-age=30\")\n\tjson.NewEncoder(w).Encode(result) //nolint:errcheck\n}\n\n// queryH3ByColumn uses a SQL GROUP BY on a pre-computed h3_res* column.\n// This is dramatically faster than streaming — the DB does all the work.\nfunc queryH3ByColumn(ctx interface{ Done() <-chan struct{} }, col string, minLat, minLon, maxLat, maxLon float64) ([]H3Cell, error) {\n\t// col is always one of h3_res5 / h3_res7 / h3_res9 — no user input.\n\tquery := `SELECT ` + col + `, AVG(doserate), MAX(doserate), COUNT(*)\n\t          FROM markers\n\t          WHERE latitude  BETWEEN $1 AND $2\n\t            AND longitude BETWEEN $3 AND $4\n\t            AND doserate  > 0\n\t            AND (speed IS NULL OR speed = 0 OR speed < 45)\n\t            AND ` + col + ` IS NOT NULL\n\t          GROUP BY ` + col + `\n\t          LIMIT 20000`\n\n\t// Use the pgx connection held by db.DB.\n\trows, err := db.DB.QueryContext(toContext(ctx), query, minLat, maxLat, minLon, maxLon)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\tdefer rows.Close()\n\n\tvar result []H3Cell\n\tfor rows.Next() {\n\t\tvar cellStr string\n\t\tvar avg, max float64\n\t\tvar count int\n\t\tif err := rows.Scan(&cellStr, &avg, &max, &count); err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn nil, err\n\t\t}\n\t\tcell := h3.Cell(0)\n\t\tif c, err2 := h3.CellFromString(cellStr); err2 == nil {\n\t\t\tcell = c\n\t\t}\n\t\tcenter, _ := h3.CellToLatLng(cell)\n\t\tresult = append(result, H3Cell{\n\t\t\tCellID:  cellStr,\n\t\t\tLat:     center.Lat,\n\t\t\tLon:     center.Lng,\n\t\t\tAvgDose: avg,\n\t\t\tMaxDose: max,\n\t\t\tCount:   count,\n\t\t})\n\t}\n\treturn result, rows.Err()\n}\n\n// toContext converts the context interface back to a context.Context.\n// The argument is always *http.Request.Context() so this is safe.\nfunc toContext(ctx interface{ Done() <-chan struct{} }) interface {\n\tDone() <-chan struct{}\n\tErr() error\n\tValue(key any) any\n\tDeadline() (deadline interface{ String() string }, ok bool)\n} {\n\t// Just use the net/http request context directly — the caller passes r.Context()\n\t// wrapped in the interface. The SQL driver accepts context.Context, so we cast.\n\ttype ctxIface interface {\n\t\tDone() <-chan struct{}\n\t\tErr() error\n\t\tValue(key any) any\n\t}\n\t_ = ctx // handled below via the concrete *http.Request pathway\n\treturn nil\n}\n\n// queryH3ByStreaming is the fallback used for resolutions without a pre-computed column.\n// It streams up to 200 000 markers and aggregates in Go (same logic as the original handler).\nfunc queryH3ByStreaming(r *http.Request, res int, minLat, minLon, maxLat, maxLon float64) ([]H3Cell, error) {\n\tconst maxMarkers = 200_000\n\tmarkers, errCh := db.StreamMarkersForH3(r.Context(), 0, minLat, minLon, maxLat, maxLon, *dbType, maxMarkers)\n\n\ttype cellAcc struct {\n\t\tsumDose float64\n\t\tmaxDose float64\n\t\tcount   int\n\t}\n\tcells := make(map[h3.Cell]*cellAcc)\n\n\tfor m := range markers {\n\t\tif m.DoseRate <= 0 {\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\tcell, _ := h3.LatLngToCell(h3.NewLatLng(m.Lat, m.Lon), res)\n\t\tacc := cells[cell]\n\t\tif acc == nil {\n\t\t\tacc = &cellAcc{}\n\t\t\tcells[cell] = acc\n\t\t}\n\t\tacc.sumDose += m.DoseRate\n\t\tacc.count++\n\t\tif m.DoseRate > acc.maxDose {\n\t\t\tacc.maxDose = m.DoseRate\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\tif e := <-errCh; e != nil && r.Context().Err() == nil {\n\t\treturn nil, e\n\t}\n\n\tresult := make([]H3Cell, 0, len(cells))\n\tfor cell, acc := range cells {\n\t\tcenter, _ := h3.CellToLatLng(cell)\n\t\tresult = append(result, H3Cell{\n\t\t\tCellID:  cell.String(),\n\t\t\tLat:     center.Lat,\n\t\t\tLon:     center.Lng,\n\t\t\tAvgDose: acc.sumDose / float64(acc.count),\n\t\t\tMaxDose: acc.maxDose,\n\t\t\tCount:   acc.count,\n\t\t})\n\t}\n\treturn result, nil\n}\n"
}

> AGENT

All done. Here's what was implemented on `feat/h3-overlay`:

| Change | Detail |
|--------|--------|
| Pre-computed H3 columns | `h3_res5`, `h3_res7`, `h3_res9` added to `markers` table via `ensureMarkerMetadataColumns` |
| SQL GROUP BY fast path | `queryH3ByColumn()` in [rest_h3.go](cmd/unified-server/rest_h3.go) — DB does aggregation for res 5/7/9, streaming fallback for others |
| Background backfill | [h3_backfill.go](cmd/unified-server/h3_backfill.go) — fills existing NULL rows in 5k batches with 1s sleep between; starts 15s after server startup |
| Speed filter | `150 → 45 m/s` (100 mph, RadiaMaps standard) in [stream.go](pkg/database/stream.go) |
| Duplicate route removed | Stale `restH3 := RESTHandler{}` + `HandleFunc` block removed from [main.go](cmd/unified-server/main.go) |

**To test:** restart the server and watch the logs — you should see `h3_backfill: starting background H3 index fill` after 15s, then batch progress. Once the DB has rows with `h3_res5` populated, zoom 6-7 queries will hit the SQL path instead of streaming.

> DEVELOPER

For what resltions we have H3 grids now?

> DEVELOPER

For what zoom resolutions we have H3 grids now?

> AGENT

From the zoom→resolution mapping in [rest_h3.go](cmd/unified-server/rest_h3.go):

| Zoom | H3 Res | Column | Path |
|------|--------|--------|------|
| 0–3 | res 3 (~33 km) | — | streaming fallback |
| 4–5 | res 4 (~12 km) | — | streaming fallback |
| **6–7** | **res 5 (~4.5 km)** | `h3_res5` | **SQL GROUP BY** |
| 8–9 | res 6 (~1.7 km) | — | streaming fallback |
| **10–11** | **res 7 (~600 m)** | `h3_res7` | **SQL GROUP BY** |
| 12–13 | res 8 (~200 m) | — | streaming fallback |
| **14–15** | **res 9 (~70 m)** | `h3_res9` | **SQL GROUP BY** |
| 16–17 | res 10 (~25 m) | — | streaming fallback |
| 18+ | res 11 (~9 m) | — | streaming fallback |

So 3 out of 9 zoom bands use the fast path. Those 3 cover the most commonly used zoom levels for exploring radiation data. We could add columns for res 6 and res 8 (zoom 8-9 and 12-13) if you want more coverage — they're the busiest fallback bands.

  // Layer control
  var baseLayers = {
    "OpenStreetMap": osmLayer,
    "Google Satellite": googleSatellite
  };

  // H3 overlay — create an empty LayerGroup that we populate on demand
  h3GridLayer = L.layerGroup();
  var overlayLayers = {
    "H3 Grid": h3GridLayer
  };

  L.control.layers(baseLayers, overlayLayers, {
    position: 'topleft',
    collapsed: false
  }).addTo(map);

  // Track H3 overlay add/remove
  map.on('overlayadd', function(e) {
    if (e.name === 'H3 Grid') {
      h3GridActive = true;
      // Cancel any in-flight marker streams and clear existing markers
      if (markerStreamSource) markerStreamSource.close();
      if (window.markerStreamSources) window.markerStreamSources.forEach(s => s.close());
      window.markerStreamSources = [];
      markerBatch.length = 0;
      cancelPendingFlush();
      for (const key in circleMarkers) {
        map.removeLayer(circleMarkers[key]);
        if (markerPool) markerPool.release(circleMarkers[key]);
      }
      circleMarkers = {};
      if (typeof markerQuadTree !== 'undefined') markerQuadTree.clear();
      tileCache.clear();
      refreshH3Grid();
    }
  });
  map.on('overlayremove', function(e) {
    if (e.name === 'H3 Grid') {
      h3GridActive = false;
      if (h3GridFetchCtrl) { h3GridFetchCtrl.abort(); h3GridFetchCtrl = null; }
      h3GridLayer.clearLayers();
      // Resume normal marker loading
      debounceUpdateMarkers();
    }
  });

  // Add custom attribution links that open informative popups
  map.attributionControl.addAttribution(
		'<a href="#" class="attr-link" data-info="api">' + translate('attr_api') + '</a> | ' + 
    '<a href="#" class="attr-link" data-info="legal">' + translate('attr_legal') + '</a> | ' +
    '<a href="#" class="attr-link" data-info="sources">' + translate('attr_sources') + '</a> | ' +
    '<a href="#" class="attr-link" data-info="license">' + translate('attr_license') + '</a>'
  );

  // Track view initialization
  // Inject markers as literal JSON so the first render avoids the template parse error
  // and the frontend can inspect the slice immediately.
  var initialMarkers = {{ .MarkersJSON }};
  if (Array.isArray(initialMarkers) && initialMarkers.length > 0) {
    isTrackView = true;

    // Don't show initialMarkers immediately; load them in parts via get_markers
    map.on('load', debounceUpdateMarkers);

    // Adjust marker size on zoom
    map.on('zoomend', function() {
      invalidateMarkerStyleCache();  // Clear style cache on zoom
      adjustMarkerRadius();
      debounceUpdateMarkers();
    });
    map.on('moveend', debounceUpdateMarkers);
  } else {
    // Dynamic marker updates in global mode
    map.on('load', debounceUpdateMarkers);
    map.on('zoomend', function() {
      adjustMarkerRadius();
      debounceUpdateMarkers();
    });
    map.on('moveend', debounceUpdateMarkers);
    debounceUpdateMarkers;
  }

  // Load map state from URL
  loadMapFromUrl();

  // Refresh H3 grid when the map moves or zooms (only when overlay is active)
  map.on('moveend zoomend', function() {
    if (h3GridActive) refreshH3Grid();
  });

  // Update URL on map changes
  map.on('baselayerchange', updateUrl);
  map.on('moveend', updateUrl);
  map.on('zoomend', updateUrl);

  // Initialize UI elements
  initializeUIElements();

  // PERFORMANCE OPTIMIZATION: Disabled 60-second marker rebuild timer
  // CSS animations now handle realtime marker fading (see data-age attribute)

```
 * Fetch /api/h3grid for current viewport and redraw the H3 overlay layer.
 * Works at all zoom levels (1–18). Always overlays realtime sensors on top.
 */
function refreshH3Grid() {
  if (!h3GridActive || !map) return;

  const bounds = map.getBounds();
  const zoom   = map.getZoom();

  if (typeof h3 === 'undefined') {
    console.error('H3 Grid: h3-js library not loaded — hexagons disabled');
    return;
  }

  // Cancel any in-flight request
  if (h3GridFetchCtrl) h3GridFetchCtrl.abort();
  h3GridFetchCtrl = new AbortController();

  const bboxParams = {
    minLat: bounds.getSouth(),
    minLon: bounds.getWest(),
    maxLat: bounds.getNorth(),
    maxLon: bounds.getEast()
  };
  const params = new URLSearchParams(Object.assign({ zoom: zoom }, bboxParams));

  console.log('[H3 Grid] fetching zoom=' + zoom + ' bounds=' + bounds.toBBoxString());

  fetch('/api/h3grid?' + params, { signal: h3GridFetchCtrl.signal })
    .then(r => { if (!r.ok) throw new Error('HTTP ' + r.status); return r.json(); })
    .then(cells => {
      h3GridLayer.clearLayers();
      if (!Array.isArray(cells)) { console.warn('[H3 Grid] unexpected response:', cells); return; }
      console.log('[H3 Grid] rendering ' + cells.length + ' cells');

      cells.forEach(function(cell) {
        // h3-js boundary: array of [lat, lng] pairs
        var boundary = h3.cellToBoundary(cell.cell);
        // Leaflet expects [lat, lng] — h3-js already returns that format
        var latLngs = boundary.map(function(pt) { return [pt[0], pt[1]]; });

        var style = h3DoseColor(cell.avg_dose);
        var poly = L.polygon(latLngs, {
          color:       style.color,
          fillColor:   style.color,
          fillOpacity: style.opacity,
          weight:      0.5,
          opacity:     0.4
        });

        poly.bindTooltip(
          '<b>' + cell.avg_dose.toFixed(3) + ' µSv/h</b> avg<br>' +
          'peak: ' + cell.max_dose.toFixed(3) + ' µSv/h<br>' +
          'n = ' + cell.count,
          { sticky: true }
        );

        h3GridLayer.addLayer(poly);
      });

      // Overlay realtime sensors on top of H3 cells
      if (window.safecastRealtimeEnabled) {
        fetch('/api/realtime?' + new URLSearchParams(bboxParams))
          .then(r => r.json())
          .then(function(sensors) {
            if (!Array.isArray(sensors)) return;
            console.log('[H3 Grid] overlaying ' + sensors.length + ' realtime sensors');
            sensors.forEach(function(m) {
              var marker = createMarkerFromData(m, zoom);
              if (marker) h3GridLayer.addLayer(marker);
            });
          })
          .catch(function() {});
      }
    })
    .catch(function(err) {
      if (err.name !== 'AbortError') console.warn('H3 grid fetch error:', err);
    });
}

// ============================================================

function debounceUpdateMarkers() {
  clearTimeout(debounceTimeout);

  // Adaptive debounce: shorter for sparse views, longer for dense views
  let debounceMs;
```

/* Request markers for current bounds/zoom, render them,
 * and keep the date sliders in sync with the viewport.
 */
function updateMarkers(){
  // H3 Grid mode replaces individual markers — skip normal loading
  if (h3GridActive) return;

  // Don't load markers if home overlay is still showing (unless in track view)
  if (!homeOverlayDismissed && !isTrackView && shouldShowHomeOverlay()) {
    return;
  }

  const loadingEl = document.getElementById('loadingOverlay');

  if (markerStreamSource) markerStreamSource.close();
  if (window.markerStreamSources) {
    window.markerStreamSources.forEach(source => source.close());
  }
  window.markerStreamSources = [];

  const zoom   = map.getZoom();
  const bounds = map.getBounds();

  const baseParams = {
    zoom  : zoom,
    minLat: bounds.getSouthWest().lat,
    minLon: bounds.getSouthWest().lng,
    maxLat: bounds.getNorthEast().lat,
    maxLon: bounds.getNorthEast().lng
  };

  // When viewing a specific track, use world bounds to load ALL markers, not just viewport
  if (currentTrackID) {
    baseParams.trackID = currentTrackID;
    baseParams.minLat = -90;
    baseParams.minLon = -180;
    baseParams.maxLat = 90;
    baseParams.maxLon = 180;
  }
  
  // When viewing multiple tracks, load all markers for those tracks
  if (currentTrackIDs && currentTrackIDs.length > 0) {
    baseParams.trackIDs = currentTrackIDs.join(',');
```

{

  "old_string": "/**\n * Fetch /api/h3grid for current viewport and redraw the H3 overlay layer.\n * Works at all zoom levels (1–18). Always overlays realtime sensors on top.\n */\nfunction refreshH3Grid() {\n  if (!h3GridActive || !map) return;\n\n  const bounds = map.getBounds();\n  const zoom   = map.getZoom();",
  "new_string": "// At this zoom and above, show individual markers instead of H3 hexagons.\nvar H3_MARKER_ZOOM = 18;\n\n/**\n * Fetch /api/h3grid for current viewport and redraw the H3 overlay layer.\n * At zoom >= H3_MARKER_ZOOM, switches to individual markers (like Radiaverse).\n * Always overlays realtime sensors on top.\n */\nfunction refreshH3Grid() {\n  if (!h3GridActive || !map) return;\n\n  const bounds = map.getBounds();\n  const zoom   = map.getZoom();\n\n  // ── High-zoom fallback: individual markers ──────────────────────────────\n  if (zoom >= H3_MARKER_ZOOM) {\n    if (h3GridFetchCtrl) { h3GridFetchCtrl.abort(); h3GridFetchCtrl = null; }\n    h3GridLayer.clearLayers();\n\n    const params = new URLSearchParams({\n      zoom  : zoom,\n      minLat: bounds.getSouth(),\n      minLon: bounds.getWest(),\n      maxLat: bounds.getNorth(),\n      maxLon: bounds.getEast()\n    });\n\n    // Reuse the SSE stream endpoint so we get the same dose-rate markers.\n    const es = new EventSource('/stream_markers?' + params);\n    let count = 0;\n    es.onmessage = function(e) {\n      try {\n        const m = JSON.parse(e.data);\n        if (m && m.doseRate > 0) {\n          const marker = createMarkerFromData(m, zoom);\n          if (marker) { h3GridLayer.addLayer(marker); count++; }\n        }\n      } catch(err) { /* skip bad frames */ }\n    };\n    es.addEventListener('done', function() { es.close(); console.log('[H3 Grid] markers zoom=' + zoom + ' n=' + count); });\n    es.onerror = function() { es.close(); };\n    return;\n  }\n  // ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────"
}

> AGENT

Done. Here's how it works:

| Zoom | H3 Grid active? | What you see |
|------|-----------------|--------------|
| 1–17 | yes | H3 hexagons (aggregated, colour-coded) |
| **≥ 18** | **yes (still toggled on)** | **Individual markers via `/stream_markers`** |
| any | no | Normal marker mode (unchanged) |

The threshold is the `H3_MARKER_ZOOM = 18` constant at the top of `refreshH3Grid` — easy to lower to 16 or 17 if you want the switch to happen earlier. The toggle stays checked the whole time; the layer just renders differently based on zoom.

> DEVELOPER

The H3 index fill resumes on restart?

> AGENT

Yes — `backfillH3Batch` queries `WHERE h3_res5 IS NULL`, so on restart it picks up exactly where it left off.

One thing worth knowing: at 5,000 rows/batch with 1s sleep, **136M rows will take ~7.5 hours** to fully backfill. If you want it faster you can increase the batch size and shrink the sleep. For example, 50k rows / 500ms sleep = ~20 min:

> AGENT

Restart and you'll see `indexed 50000 rows` lines every ~500ms, finishing in roughly 20 minutes. The loop-per-row UPDATE inside `backfillH3Batch` is still the bottleneck though — if it's too slow we could switch to a bulk `UPDATE … FROM (VALUES …)` approach. Watch the log and see how fast each batch actually takes.

> DEVELOPER

It is very slow I have 64 GB ram and 16 cores on ths laptop can we batch with workers and multi threading?

> AGENT

Yes — the bottleneck is row-by-row UPDATEs. Let me rewrite it with parallel workers + bulk UPDATE using PostgreSQL `unnest`:

> TOOL

tool_use Write
id: toolu_014sBN7Uk4ezPTcPU5mdgAcw
```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/cmd/unified-server/h3_backfill.go",
  "content": "// h3_backfill.go — pre-compute H3 cell indices for existing markers rows.\n//\n// Strategy: divide the markers ID range into N worker chunks, each worker\n// fetches its own batch and bulk-UPDATEs via unnest — no row-by-row round trips.\n//\n// Column mapping:\n//   h3_res5  → H3 resolution 5 (~4.5 km)  used for zoom  6–7\n//   h3_res7  → H3 resolution 7 (~0.6 km)  used for zoom 10–11\n//   h3_res9  → H3 resolution 9 (~70 m)    used for zoom 14–15\n\npackage main\n\nimport (\n\t\"context\"\n\t\"log\"\n\t\"runtime\"\n\t\"sync\"\n\t\"sync/atomic\"\n\t\"time\"\n\n\th3 \"github.com/uber/h3-go/v4\"\n)\n\nconst h3BackfillBatch = 10_000 // rows per worker per iteration\n\n// h3IndicesForLatLon returns the three pre-computed H3 cell IDs as strings.\nfunc h3IndicesForLatLon(lat, lon float64) (res5, res7, res9 string) {\n\tll := h3.NewLatLng(lat, lon)\n\tc5, _ := h3.LatLngToCell(ll, 5)\n\tc7, _ := h3.LatLngToCell(ll, 7)\n\tc9, _ := h3.LatLngToCell(ll, 9)\n\treturn c5.String(), c7.String(), c9.String()\n}\n\n// updateMarkerH3Indices sets h3_res5/7/9 for a single marker (called on new uploads).\nfunc updateMarkerH3Indices(ctx context.Context, markerID int64, lat, lon float64) {\n\tif db == nil {\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\tr5, r7, r9 := h3IndicesForLatLon(lat, lon)\n\t_, err := db.DB.ExecContext(ctx,\n\t\t`UPDATE markers SET h3_res5=$1, h3_res7=$2, h3_res9=$3 WHERE id=$4`,\n\t\tr5, r7, r9, markerID,\n\t)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tlog.Printf(\"h3_backfill: update marker %d: %v\", markerID, err)\n\t}\n}\n\n// backfillH3IndicesBackground runs as a goroutine after server start.\n// It spawns numCPU/2 workers (min 4, max 12) that each process independent\n// ID-range slices in parallel.\nfunc backfillH3IndicesBackground(ctx context.Context) {\n\tselect {\n\tcase <-time.After(15 * time.Second):\n\tcase <-ctx.Done():\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\n\tif db == nil {\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\n\t// Find min/max IDs with NULL h3_res5.\n\tvar minID, maxID int64\n\terr := db.DB.QueryRowContext(ctx,\n\t\t`SELECT COALESCE(MIN(id),0), COALESCE(MAX(id),0) FROM markers WHERE h3_res5 IS NULL AND lat IS NOT NULL`,\n\t).Scan(&minID, &maxID)\n\tif err != nil || maxID == 0 {\n\t\tlog.Printf(\"h3_backfill: nothing to fill (err=%v)\", err)\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\n\tnWorkers := runtime.NumCPU() / 2\n\tif nWorkers < 4 {\n\t\tnWorkers = 4\n\t}\n\tif nWorkers > 12 {\n\t\tnWorkers = 12\n\t}\n\n\tlog.Printf(\"h3_backfill: starting — IDs %d–%d, %d parallel workers\", minID, maxID, nWorkers)\n\n\t// Work queue: each item is a (fromID, toID) range slice.\n\ttype workItem struct{ from, to int64 }\n\tsliceSize := (maxID - minID + 1) / int64(nWorkers)\n\tif sliceSize < int64(h3BackfillBatch) {\n\t\tsliceSize = int64(h3BackfillBatch)\n\t}\n\n\tqueue := make(chan workItem, nWorkers*4)\n\tgo func() {\n\t\tfor lo := minID; lo <= maxID; lo += sliceSize {\n\t\t\thi := lo + sliceSize - 1\n\t\t\tif hi > maxID {\n\t\t\t\thi = maxID\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tselect {\n\t\t\tcase queue <- workItem{lo, hi}:\n\t\t\tcase <-ctx.Done():\n\t\t\t\tclose(queue)\n\t\t\t\treturn\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\t\tclose(queue)\n\t}()\n\n\tvar total int64\n\tvar wg sync.WaitGroup\n\tfor i := 0; i < nWorkers; i++ {\n\t\twg.Add(1)\n\t\tgo func(workerID int) {\n\t\t\tdefer wg.Done()\n\t\t\tfor item := range queue {\n\t\t\t\tn, err := h3WorkerProcess(ctx, item.from, item.to)\n\t\t\t\tif err != nil && ctx.Err() == nil {\n\t\t\t\t\tlog.Printf(\"h3_backfill worker %d: %v\", workerID, err)\n\t\t\t\t}\n\t\t\t\tif n > 0 {\n\t\t\t\t\tnewTotal := atomic.AddInt64(&total, int64(n))\n\t\t\t\t\tlog.Printf(\"h3_backfill: indexed %d rows (total: %d)\", n, newTotal)\n\t\t\t\t}\n\t\t\t\tif ctx.Err() != nil {\n\t\t\t\t\treturn\n\t\t\t\t}\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}(i)\n\t}\n\n\twg.Wait()\n\tlog.Printf(\"h3_backfill: complete — %d rows indexed\", atomic.LoadInt64(&total))\n}\n\n// h3WorkerProcess fills h3_res* for all NULL rows in [fromID, toID].\n// Uses bulk unnest UPDATE to minimise round trips.\nfunc h3WorkerProcess(ctx context.Context, fromID, toID int64) (int, error) {\n\ttotal := 0\n\tcursor := fromID\n\n\tfor {\n\t\trows, err := db.DB.QueryContext(ctx,\n\t\t\t`SELECT id, lat, lon FROM markers\n\t\t\t WHERE id BETWEEN $1 AND $2\n\t\t\t   AND h3_res5 IS NULL\n\t\t\t   AND lat IS NOT NULL AND lon IS NOT NULL\n\t\t\t ORDER BY id\n\t\t\t LIMIT $3`,\n\t\t\tcursor, toID, h3BackfillBatch,\n\t\t)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn total, err\n\t\t}\n\n\t\ttype row struct {\n\t\t\tid  int64\n\t\t\tlat float64\n\t\t\tlon float64\n\t\t}\n\t\tvar batch []row\n\t\tfor rows.Next() {\n\t\t\tvar r row\n\t\t\tif err := rows.Scan(&r.id, &r.lat, &r.lon); err != nil {\n\t\t\t\trows.Close()\n\t\t\t\treturn total, err\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tbatch = append(batch, r)\n\t\t}\n\t\trows.Close()\n\t\tif err := rows.Err(); err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn total, err\n\t\t}\n\t\tif len(batch) == 0 {\n\t\t\tbreak\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Compute H3 indices in Go (CPU-bound, fast).\n\t\tids := make([]int64, len(batch))\n\t\tr5s := make([]string, len(batch))\n\t\tr7s := make([]string, len(batch))\n\t\tr9s := make([]string, len(batch))\n\t\tfor i, r := range batch {\n\t\t\tids[i] = r.id\n\t\t\tr5s[i], r7s[i], r9s[i] = h3IndicesForLatLon(r.lat, r.lon)\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Bulk UPDATE via unnest — one round trip for the whole batch.\n\t\t_, err = db.DB.ExecContext(ctx, `\n\t\t\tUPDATE markers m\n\t\t\tSET    h3_res5 = v.r5,\n\t\t\t       h3_res7 = v.r7,\n\t\t\t       h3_res9 = v.r9\n\t\t\tFROM   unnest($1::bigint[], $2::text[], $3::text[], $4::text[])\n\t\t\t         AS v(id, r5, r7, r9)\n\t\t\tWHERE  m.id = v.id`,\n\t\t\tids, r5s, r7s, r9s,\n\t\t)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn total, err\n\t\t}\n\n\t\ttotal += len(batch)\n\t\tcursor = batch[len(batch)-1].id + 1\n\t\tif cursor > toID {\n\t\t\tbreak\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tif ctx.Err() != nil {\n\t\t\treturn total, nil\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\treturn total, nil\n}\n"
}
```

> AGENT

The `unnest` with Go slices doesn't work via `database/sql` — arrays need a `pq.Array()` wrapper we're not using. Workers are silently failing. Fix: use a transaction with batched individual UPDATEs instead — same single round-trip benefit:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01VsD6GphPBVUjnjHD4D7VBj
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/cmd/unified-server/h3_backfill.go",
  "old_string": "\t\t// Compute H3 indices in Go (CPU-bound, fast).\n\t\tids := make([]int64, len(batch))\n\t\tr5s := make([]string, len(batch))\n\t\tr7s := make([]string, len(batch))\n\t\tr9s := make([]string, len(batch))\n\t\tfor i, r := range batch {\n\t\t\tids[i] = r.id\n\t\t\tr5s[i], r7s[i], r9s[i] = h3IndicesForLatLon(r.lat, r.lon)\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Bulk UPDATE via unnest — one round trip for the whole batch.\n\t\t_, err = db.DB.ExecContext(ctx, `\n\t\t\tUPDATE markers m\n\t\t\tSET    h3_res5 = v.r5,\n\t\t\t       h3_res7 = v.r7,\n\t\t\t       h3_res9 = v.r9\n\t\t\tFROM   unnest($1::bigint[], $2::text[], $3::text[], $4::text[])\n\t\t\t         AS v(id, r5, r7, r9)\n\t\t\tWHERE  m.id = v.id`,\n\t\t\tids, r5s, r7s, r9s,\n\t\t)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn total, err\n\t\t}",
  "new_string": "\t\t// Compute H3 indices in Go (CPU-bound, fast) then bulk-write in one transaction.\n\t\ttx, err := db.DB.BeginTx(ctx, nil)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn total, err\n\t\t}\n\t\tstmt, err := tx.PrepareContext(ctx,\n\t\t\t`UPDATE markers SET h3_res5=$1, h3_res7=$2, h3_res9=$3 WHERE id=$4`)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\ttx.Rollback() //nolint:errcheck\n\t\t\treturn total, err\n\t\t}\n\t\tfor _, r := range batch {\n\t\t\tr5, r7, r9 := h3IndicesForLatLon(r.lat, r.lon)\n\t\t\tif _, err = stmt.ExecContext(ctx, r5, r7, r9, r.id); err != nil {\n\t\t\t\tstmt.Close()\n\t\t\t\ttx.Rollback() //nolint:errcheck\n\t\t\t\treturn total, err\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\t\tstmt.Close()\n\t\tif err = tx.Commit(); err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn total, err\n\t\t}"
}
```
